     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh — render a read-only dashboard from ROADMAP.md (GH-27).
     3	
     4	set -euo pipefail
     5	
     6	HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
     7	# GH-272: releases-merge-resolve.sh drives this renderer against a --root TARGET repo —
     8	# the ledger it renders and the dashboard it writes must follow the target, not this
     9	# script's own checkout. Self-location stays the default for every other caller.
    10	ROOT="${ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_ROOT:-$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)}"
    11	SOURCE_FILE="${ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_SOURCE:-"$ROOT/ROADMAP.md"}"
    12	OUTPUT_FILE="${ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_OUTPUT:-"$ROOT/ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md"}"
    13	
    14	usage() {
    15	  cat <<'EOF'
    16	Usage: bash utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh [--check]
    17	
    18	  Default: render ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md from the canonical root ROADMAP.md.
    19	  --check: exit 0 when ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md is in sync; non-zero on drift.
    20	EOF
    21	}
    22	
    23	MODE="write"
    24	if (($# > 1)); then
    25	  usage
    26	  exit 2
    27	fi
    28	if (($# == 1)); then
    29	  case "$1" in
    30	    --check) MODE="check" ;;
    31	    --help|-h) usage; exit 0 ;;
    32	    *) usage; exit 2 ;;
    33	  esac
    34	fi
    35	
    36	command -v node >/dev/null 2>&1 || {
    37	  echo "roadmap-dashboard: node is required" >&2
    38	  exit 2
    39	}
    40	
    41	TMP_DIR="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/roadmap-dashboard.XXXXXX")"
    42	trap 'rm -rf "$TMP_DIR"' EXIT
    43	RENDERED="$TMP_DIR/ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md"
    44	
    45	# GH-243: in a releases-mode repo the DB is the roadmap's source of truth and ROADMAP.md is
    46	# frozen — render from `roadmap list --json` (each row's lossless raw_text under its section)
    47	# instead of the legacy file. An explicit ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_SOURCE still wins (test seam), and
    48	# any failure here falls back to the legacy file rather than emitting an empty dashboard.
    49	if [ -z "${ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_SOURCE:-}" ] \
    50	   && (grep -q "ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases" "$ROOT/.pdda-mode" 2>/dev/null || [ ! -f "$ROOT/ROADMAP.md" ]) \
    51	   && [ -f "$ROOT/releases.db" ] && command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    52	  DB_SRC="$TMP_DIR/roadmap-from-db.md"
    53	  DB_JSON="$TMP_DIR/roadmap-rows.json"
    54	  # JSON goes through a temp file passed as argv — `python3 -` takes its PROGRAM from the
    55	  # heredoc on stdin, so piping data in as well would feed the parser nothing (the PR #240
    56	  # rollup.sh bug, same shape). The APP resolves from HERE (this script's own checkout) while
    57	  # --root follows $ROOT: under ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_ROOT (GH-272) the target repo may ship the
    58	  # ledger without a utils/ tree at all.
    59	  if python3 "$HERE/py/releases_app.py" --root "$ROOT" roadmap list --json > "$DB_JSON" 2>/dev/null \
    60	     && python3 - "$DB_JSON" > "$DB_SRC" <<'PY'
    61	import json, sys
    62	with open(sys.argv[1]) as f:
    63	    rows = json.load(f)
    64	print("# ROADMAP (rendered from the RELEASES DB — GH-243; ROADMAP.md is frozen legacy)\n")
    65	print("## Ledger\n")   # the renderer only counts sections below this heading
    66	sections = {}
    67	for r in rows:
    68	    sections.setdefault(r.get("section") or "(unsectioned)", []).append(r)
    69	for section, items in sections.items():
    70	    print(f"### {section}\n")
    71	    for r in sorted(items, key=lambda x: x.get("position") or 0):
    72	        line = (r.get("raw_text") or "").strip()
    73	        if not line:
    74	            gh = f"GH-{r['gh_number']} · " if r.get("gh_number") is not None else ""
    75	            line = f"- **{gh}{r.get('title', '')}**"
    76	        print(line)
    77	    print()
    78	PY
    79	  then
    80	    [ -s "$DB_SRC" ] && SOURCE_FILE="$DB_SRC"
    81	  fi
    82	fi
    83	
    84	if [ ! -f "$SOURCE_FILE" ]; then
    85	  echo "roadmap-dashboard: source not found: $SOURCE_FILE" >&2
    86	  exit 3
    87	fi
    88	
    89	node - "$SOURCE_FILE" >"$RENDERED" <<'NODE'
    90	const fs = require("fs");
    91	
    92	const sourcePath = process.argv[2];
    93	const raw = fs.readFileSync(sourcePath, "utf8");
    94	const lines = raw.split(/\r?\n/);
    95	
    96	const banner = "<!-- GENERATED by utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh — DO NOT EDIT; edit ROADMAP.md -->";
    97	const ledgerSections = [
    98	  "Queue / parked intake",
    99	  "Queue",              // GH-243: early `releases roadmap add` rows parked under the short name
   100	  "In progress",
   101	  "Completed",
   102	  "Deferred · vision",
   103	];
   104	const knownStatuses = ["🟢", "🟡", "⏸️", "⛔", "✅", "🔮", "🔲"];
   105	const extraStatuses = ["⚠️"];
   106	
   107	function escapeCell(value) {
   108	  return String(value)
   109	    .replace(/\|/g, "\\|")
   110	    .replace(/</g, "&lt;")
   111	    .replace(/>/g, "&gt;");
   112	}
   113	
   114	function stripMarkdown(value) {
   115	  return value
   116	    .replace(/`([^`]+)`/g, "$1")
   117	    .replace(/\*\*([^*]+)\*\*/g, "$1")
   118	    .replace(/\*([^*]+)\*/g, "$1")
   119	    .replace(/\[([^\]]+)\]\(([^)]+)\)/g, "$1")
   120	    .trim();
   121	}
   122	
   123	function parseBullet(block) {
   124	  const text = block.map((line) => line.trim()).join(" ").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
   125	  const titleMatch = text.match(/^- \*\*(.+?)\*\*/);
   126	  const title = titleMatch ? stripMarkdown(titleMatch[1]) : stripMarkdown(text.replace(/^- /, ""));
   127	
   128	  let status = "—";
   129	  for (const emoji of knownStatuses.concat(extraStatuses)) {
   130	    if (text.includes(emoji)) {
   131	      status = emoji;
   132	      break;
   133	    }
   134	  }
   135	
   136	  const links = [];
   137	  const linkPattern = /\[([^\]]+)\]\(([^)]+)\)/g;
   138	  let match;
   139	  while ((match = linkPattern.exec(text)) !== null) {
   140	    links.push(`[${match[1]}](${match[2]})`);
   141	  }
   142	
   143	  return { title, status, links };
   144	}
   145	
   146	function formatTally(items) {
   147	  const counts = new Map();
   148	  for (const emoji of knownStatuses) counts.set(emoji, 0);
   149	  const extras = [];
   150	
   151	  for (const item of items) {
   152	    if (counts.has(item.status)) {
   153	      counts.set(item.status, counts.get(item.status) + 1);
   154	      continue;
   155	    }
   156	    if (item.status !== "—") {
   157	      if (!counts.has(item.status)) extras.push(item.status);
   158	      counts.set(item.status, (counts.get(item.status) || 0) + 1);
   159	    }
   160	  }
   161	
   162	  const ordered = knownStatuses.slice();
   163	  for (const emoji of extras) {
   164	    if (!ordered.includes(emoji)) ordered.push(emoji);
   165	  }
   166	
   167	  return ordered.map((emoji) => `${emoji} ${counts.get(emoji) || 0}`).join(" · ");
   168	}
   169	
   170	const sections = new Map();
   171	for (const heading of ledgerSections) sections.set(heading, []);
   172	
   173	let inLedger = false;
   174	let currentSection = null;
   175	const droppedRows = [];
   176	
   177	for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
   178	  const line = lines[i];
   179	
   180	  if (!inLedger) {
   181	    if (/^##\s+Ledger\s*$/.test(line.trim())) inLedger = true;
   182	    continue;
   183	  }
   184	
   185	  if (/^##\s+/.test(line) && !/^##\s+Ledger\s*$/.test(line.trim())) break;
   186	
   187	  const sectionMatch = line.match(/^###\s+(.+?)\s*$/);
   188	  if (sectionMatch) {
   189	    const heading = sectionMatch[1].trim();
   190	    currentSection = sections.has(heading) ? heading : null;
   191	    continue;
   192	  }
   193	
   194	  if (!currentSection) continue;
   195	
   196	  const trimmed = line.trim();
   197	  if (!trimmed || trimmed.startsWith("<!--") || trimmed.startsWith("#")) continue;
   198	
   199	  if (!/^- \*\*[^\r\n*]+?\*\*/.test(line)) {
   200	    // GH-257: track non-empty dropped/unparseable line under an active ledger section
   201	    const issueMatch = trimmed.match(/(?:GH-?|#)(\d+)/i);
   202	    const id = issueMatch ? `#${issueMatch[1]}` : JSON.stringify(trimmed.length > 30 ? trimmed.slice(0, 30) + "..." : trimmed);
   203	    droppedRows.push({ section: currentSection, id, line: trimmed });
   204	    continue;
   205	  }
   206	
   207	  const block = [line];
   208	  while (i + 1 < lines.length) {
   209	    const next = lines[i + 1];
   210	    if (/^###\s+/.test(next) || /^##\s+/.test(next) || /^\s*[-*+]\s+/.test(next)) break;
   211	    block.push(next);
   212	    i += 1;
   213	  }
   214	  sections.get(currentSection).push(parseBullet(block));
   215	}
   216	
   217	if (droppedRows.length > 0) {
   218	  const ids = droppedRows.map((d) => d.id).join(", ");
   219	  process.stderr.write(`roadmap-dashboard: warning: dropped ${droppedRows.length} unparseable row(s): ${ids}\n`);
   220	}
   221	
   222	const output = [];
   223	output.push(banner);
   224	output.push("");
   225	output.push("# ROADMAP Dashboard");
   226	output.push("");
   227	output.push("Read-only derived view of the root [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) ledger.");
   228	output.push("");
   229	
   230	for (const heading of ledgerSections) {
   231	  const items = sections.get(heading) || [];
   232	  output.push(`## ${heading}`);
   233	  output.push("");
   234	  output.push(`Summary: ${items.length} items | Tally: ${formatTally(items)}`);
   235	  output.push("");
   236	  output.push("| Item | Status | Links |");
   237	  output.push("| --- | --- | --- |");
   238	  for (const item of items) {
   239	    const links = item.links.length > 0 ? item.links.join(" · ") : "—";
   240	    output.push(`| ${escapeCell(item.title)} | ${escapeCell(item.status)} | ${links} |`);
   241	  }
   242	  if (items.length === 0) {
   243	    output.push("| — | — | — |");
   244	  }
   245	  output.push("");
   246	}
   247	
   248	process.stdout.write(output.join("\n"));
   249	NODE
   250	
   251	if [[ "$MODE" == "check" ]]; then
   252	  if [[ ! -f "$OUTPUT_FILE" ]]; then
   253	    echo "roadmap-dashboard: missing artifact: $OUTPUT_FILE" >&2
   254	    exit 1
   255	  fi
   256	  if cmp -s "$RENDERED" "$OUTPUT_FILE"; then
   257	    echo "roadmap-dashboard: artifact is in sync"
   258	    exit 0
   259	  fi
   260	  echo "roadmap-dashboard: artifact drift detected: $OUTPUT_FILE" >&2
   261	  diff -u "$OUTPUT_FILE" "$RENDERED" || true
   262	  exit 1
   263	fi
   264	
   265	cp "$RENDERED" "$OUTPUT_FILE"
   266	echo "roadmap-dashboard: wrote $OUTPUT_FILE"
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# utils/leaderboard.sh — render LEADERBOARD.md, the ranked read of every rated task (GH-108).
     3	#
     4	# ONE SCORER, NEVER TWO. This script does NOT compute calc and does NOT apply the
     5	# override-wins-over-calc rule. It consumes `export_timeline.py --json` and sorts on the
     6	# `effectiveScore` the exporter already derived. A shell script re-deriving scores would be the
     7	# exact drift class the "calc is never stored" rule exists to prevent, one layer up — the whole
     8	# job here is sort, rank, format.
     9	#
    10	# LEADERBOARD.md is GENERATED and never hand-edited, following the established
    11	# ROADMAP.md -> utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh -> ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md pattern: committed so ranking
    12	# changes show up in diffs and review, greppable by agents that never open a browser, readable on
    13	# GitHub. Regeneration is idempotent — a stale checked-in copy is a review signal, not a hazard.
    14	
    15	set -euo pipefail
    16	
    17	HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
    18	ROOT="$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)"
    19	DB="${LEADERBOARD_DB:-$ROOT/releases.db}"
    20	OUT="${LEADERBOARD_OUTPUT:-$ROOT/LEADERBOARD.md}"
    21	EXPORTER="$ROOT/utils/timeline/export_timeline.py"
    22	
    23	usage() {
    24	  cat <<'EOF'
    25	Usage: bash utils/leaderboard.sh [--check] [--top N]
    26	
    27	  Default: render LEADERBOARD.md from releases.db via export_timeline.py --json.
    28	  --check: write nothing; exit 0 when LEADERBOARD.md is already in sync, 1 on drift.
    29	  --top N: also print the top N to stdout (default: print nothing extra).
    30	
    31	Env: LEADERBOARD_DB, LEADERBOARD_OUTPUT.
    32	EOF
    33	}
    34	
    35	MODE="write"
    36	TOP=0
    37	while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
    38	  case "$1" in
    39	    --check) MODE="check" ;;
    40	    --top) TOP="${2:-5}"; shift ;;
    41	    -h|--help) usage; exit 0 ;;
    42	    *) echo "leaderboard: unknown argument $1" >&2; usage >&2; exit 2 ;;
    43	  esac
    44	  shift
    45	done
    46	
    47	[ -f "$DB" ] || { echo "leaderboard: no releases DB at $DB" >&2; exit 2; }
    48	[ -f "$EXPORTER" ] || { echo "leaderboard: no exporter at $EXPORTER" >&2; exit 2; }
    49	
    50	# The payload goes through a FILE, not a pipe: `python3 - <<HEREDOC` already takes the script
    51	# from stdin, so a piped payload would arrive on an exhausted stream.
    52	PAYLOAD="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/leaderboard.XXXXXX.json")"
    53	trap 'rm -f "$PAYLOAD"' EXIT
    54	python3 "$EXPORTER" --db "$DB" --json > "$PAYLOAD"
    55	
    56	RENDERED="$(python3 - "$PAYLOAD" "$TOP" <<'PY'
    57	import json, sys
    58	
    59	payload = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))
    60	top_n = int(sys.argv[2])
    61	
    62	# ratedTasks is the complete, already-ranked set the exporter emits: every rated roadmap row
    63	# once,
    64	# annotated with where it currently sits. Ranking off the release cards alone would silently omit
    65	# every rated task still in the queue — most of the candidates for "what goes to the front".
    66	rows = [{
    67	    "id": t["id"],
    68	    "title": t["title"],
    69	    "release": t["release"] or "—",
    70	    "lane": t["lane"],
    71	    "score": t["metrics"]["effectiveScore"],
    72	    "calc": t["metrics"]["calc"],
    73	    "override": (t.get("override") or {}).get("value"),
    74	    "axes": (t["metrics"]["pri"], t["metrics"]["sev"],
    75	             t["metrics"]["appeal"], t["metrics"]["effort"]),
    76	    "issue": (t.get("links") or {}).get("issue"),
    77	} for t in payload["ratedTasks"]]
    78	
    79	out = []
    80	w = out.append
    81	w("<!-- GENERATED by utils/leaderboard.sh from releases.db — do not hand-edit. -->")
    82	w("")
    83	w("# Leaderboard")
    84	w("")
    85	w("Every rated task in the ledger, highest first. **Higher is better on every axis**, effort")
    86	w("included — it scores cheapness, not cost, so a 90/90/90/90 item reads as what it is: a")
    87	w("screaming quick win. `calc` is the equal-weighted sum of the four axes (4–400) and is derived,")
    88	w("never stored. `ovr` is the operator override; where it exists it replaces `calc` for ranking,")
    89	w("while the four axes keep their honest values underneath.")
    90	w("")
    91	if not rows:
    92	    w("_No rated tasks yet. Add `rated N/N/N/N` to a ROADMAP.md entry and run "
    93	      "`releases roadmap sync`._")
    94	else:
    95	    w("| # | score | task | release | lane | pri | sev | appeal | effort | calc | ovr |")
    96	    w("|--:|------:|------|---------|------|----:|----:|-------:|-------:|-----:|----:|")
    97	    for i, r in enumerate(rows, 1):
    98	        title = r["title"].replace("|", "\\|")
    99	        label = "[%s](%s)" % (r["id"], r["issue"]) if r["issue"] else r["id"]
   100	        w("| %d | **%d** | %s — %s | %s | %s | %d | %d | %d | %d | %d | %s |"
   101	          % (i, r["score"], label, title, r["release"], r["lane"],
   102	             r["axes"][0], r["axes"][1], r["axes"][2], r["axes"][3], r["calc"],
   103	             str(r["override"]) if r["override"] is not None else "—"))
   104	    w("")
   105	    w("**Top of the line:** %s — %s (score %d%s)."
   106	      % (rows[0]["id"], rows[0]["title"], rows[0]["score"],
   107	         ", operator override" if rows[0]["override"] is not None else ""))
   108	w("")
   109	w("Source: `releases.db` via `export_timeline.py --json`. Regenerate with "
   110	  "`bash utils/leaderboard.sh`.")
   111	
   112	if top_n:
   113	    for r in rows[:top_n]:
   114	        print("%d\t%s\t%s" % (r["score"], r["id"], r["title"]), file=sys.stderr)
   115	print("\n".join(out))
   116	PY
   117	)"
   118	
   119	if [ "$MODE" = "check" ]; then
   120	  if [ -f "$OUT" ] && [ "$RENDERED" = "$(cat "$OUT")" ]; then
   121	    echo "leaderboard: $OUT is in sync"
   122	    exit 0
   123	  fi
   124	  echo "leaderboard: $OUT is stale — run 'bash utils/leaderboard.sh'" >&2
   125	  exit 1
   126	fi
   127	
   128	printf '%s\n' "$RENDERED" > "$OUT"
   129	echo "leaderboard: wrote $OUT"
  1145	
  1146	def _stage_write(final_path, content):
  1147	    """Staged temp-name write; the caller renames (atomic) only once the DB commit is durable."""
  1148	    tmp = "%s.tmp-%s" % (final_path, new_txn_id()[:12])
  1149	    with open(tmp, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as fh:
  1150	        fh.write(content)
  1151	        fh.flush()
  1152	        os.fsync(fh.fileno())
  1153	    return tmp
  1154	
  1155	
  1156	def _atomic_write(final_path, content):
  1157	    tmp = _stage_write(final_path, content)
  1158	    os.replace(tmp, final_path)
  1159	
  1160	
  1161	# ── the writer protocol (PRD Git story 2-3): journal -> txn -> stage -> rename -> clear ─────────
  1162	
  1163	def refresh_preview(root):
  1164	    """GH-106: after a successful write, refresh the baked viewer artifacts — but only the ones
  1165	    the repo has ADOPTED (the file already exists at root; presence is the opt-in signal, so
  1166	    fixtures and non-adopting repos are silent no-ops). Best-effort by design: the write is
  1167	    already durable with its receipt when this runs, so a refresh failure WARNS on stderr and
  1168	    never fails the operation. Success is silent (the exporter's stdout is captured).
  1169	
  1170	    GH-108 adds LEADERBOARD.html on the same terms — one template, two baked artifacts, both
  1171	    regenerated by the same hook so a ranking can never lag the ledger it ranks."""
  1172	    exporter = os.path.join(root, "utils", "timeline", "export_timeline.py")
  1173	    if not os.path.exists(exporter):
  1174	        return
  1175	    for name, flag in (("RELEASES-PREVIEW.html", "--preview"),
  1176	                       ("LEADERBOARD.html", "--leaderboard")):
  1177	        target = os.path.join(root, name)
  1178	        if not os.path.exists(target):
  1179	            continue
  1180	        try:
  1181	            r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, exporter, flag, target],
  1182	                               cwd=root, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=60)
  1183	            if r.returncode != 0:
  1184	                print("warning: %s refresh failed (the write itself is committed): %s"
  1185	                      % (name, r.stderr.strip() or r.stdout.strip() or
  1186	                         "exit %d" % r.returncode), file=sys.stderr)
  1187	        except Exception as exc:  # never let a rendered artifact break a committed write
  1188	            print("warning: %s refresh failed (the write itself is committed): %s"
  1189	                  % (name, exc), file=sys.stderr)
  1190	    # LEADERBOARD.md rides the same adoption signal — generated, never hand-edited.
  1191	    board = os.path.join(root, "LEADERBOARD.md")
  1192	    script = os.path.join(root, "utils", "leaderboard.sh")
  1193	    if os.path.exists(board) and os.path.exists(script):
  1194	        try:
  1195	            r = subprocess.run(["bash", script], cwd=root, capture_output=True, text=True,
  1196	                               timeout=60)
  1197	            if r.returncode != 0:
  1198	                print("warning: LEADERBOARD.md refresh failed (the write itself is committed): %s"
  1199	                      % (r.stderr.strip() or "exit %d" % r.returncode), file=sys.stderr)
  1200	        except Exception as exc:
  1201	            print("warning: LEADERBOARD.md refresh failed (the write itself is committed): %s"
  1202	                  % exc, file=sys.stderr)
  1203	
  1204	
  1205	def perform_write(root, conn, op, target_gid, mutate):
  1206	    """Run one CLI transaction under the full multi-artifact protocol:
  1207	
  1208	      write intent journal (txn_id, NEXT generation, planned outputs)   [BEFORE the DB commit]
  1209	      -> BEGIN IMMEDIATE -> mutate -> stamp generation into settings -> COMMIT
  1210	      -> stage dump (+ generated view when one exists), each carrying that generation
  1211	      -> atomic renames -> clear journal.
  1212	
  1213	    `mutate(conn)` does the business writes; the op_receipt (before/after business-state digests)
  1214	    is appended HERE so no writer can forget it. Recovery per boundary is `check`'s job
  1215	    (recover_from_journal); RELEASES_APP_CRASH_AT lands on the five named boundaries. The caller
  1216	    must NOT hold the writer lock (this function takes it)."""
  1217	    paths = artifact_paths(root)
  1218	    lock = WriterLock(root)
  1219	    lock.acquire()
  1220	    try:
  1221	        if os.path.exists(lock.journal_path):
  1222	            refuse("journal-live",
  1223	                   "an intent journal from an interrupted write exists (%s); run `releases check` "
  1224	                   "to recover before writing again" % lock.journal_path)
  1225	
  3510	                print("  + [%s] %s" % (e["section"], e["title"][:70]))
  3511	            for row, e in updates:
  3512	                print("  ~ [%s] %s" % (e["section"], e["title"][:70]))
  3513	            for row in removes:
  3514	                print("  - [%s] %s" % (row["section"], row["title"][:70]))
  3515	            return
  3516	
  3517	        # The row MIRRORS the entry text: converting an entry from cx/risk/eff to `rated` populates
  3518	        # the rating columns and NULLs the legacy ones in the SAME sync. A mirror that disagreed
  3519	        # with its source would re-update on every sync forever; the entry's lossless raw_text is
  3520	        # the historical record, not the row.

  3524	            _ensure_roadmap_schema(c)
  3525	            ts = now_iso()
  3526	            ins_cols = ["global_id", "repo_id"] + fields + ["first_seen", "updated_at"]
  3527	            ins_sql = "INSERT INTO roadmap_items(%s) VALUES (%s)" % (
  3528	                ", ".join(ins_cols), ", ".join("?" for _ in ins_cols))
  3529	            upd_sql = "UPDATE roadmap_items SET %s, updated_at=? WHERE id=?" % (
  3530	                ", ".join("%s=?" % f for f in fields))
  3531	            for e in adds:
  3532	                c.execute(ins_sql,
  3533	                          [new_gid("rmi-"), repo["id"]] + [e[f] for f in fields] + [ts, ts])
  3534	            for row, e in updates:
  3535	                c.execute(upd_sql, [e[f] for f in fields] + [ts, row["id"]])
  3536	            for row in removes:
  3537	                c.execute("DELETE FROM roadmap_items WHERE id = ?", (row["id"],))
  3538	
  3539	        txn = perform_write(root, conn, "roadmap-sync", None, mutate)
  3540	        print(summary + " (txn %s, generation %d)" % (txn[:12], get_generation(conn)))
  3541	    finally:
  3542	        conn.close()
  3543	
  3544	
  3545	def cmd_roadmap_list(args):
  3546	    root = resolve_root(args.root)
  3547	    conn = connect(artifact_paths(root)["db"])
  3548	    try:
  3549	        if getattr(args, "as_json", False):
  3550	            # Machine-readable contract for consumers (hq rollup): the human rendering below is
  3551	            # a display, not an API — its column offsets shift whenever a field widens.
  3552	            rows = []
  3553	            if _table_exists(conn, "roadmap_items"):
  3554	                for r in conn.execute("SELECT * FROM roadmap_items ORDER BY section, position"):
  3555	                    rows.append({"global_id": r["global_id"], "gh_number": r["gh_number"],
  3556	                                 "title": r["title"], "section": r["section"],
  3557	                                 "position": r["position"], "status_marker": r["status_marker"],
  3558	                                 "doc_path": _col(r, "doc_path"), "issue_url": _col(r, "issue_url"),
  3559	                                 "raw_text": _col(r, "raw_text")})
  3560	            print(json.dumps(rows, ensure_ascii=False))
  3561	            return
  3562	        if not _table_exists(conn, "roadmap_items"):
  3563	            print("(no roadmap shadow yet — run `releases roadmap sync`)")
  3564	            return
  3565	        for r in conn.execute("""SELECT * FROM roadmap_items ORDER BY section, position"""):
  3566	            gh = ("GH-%d" % r["gh_number"]) if r["gh_number"] is not None else "-"
  3567	            cre = ("%s/%s/%s" % (r["complexity"], r["risk"], r["effort"])
  3568	                   if r["complexity"] is not None else "-")
  3569	            # calc is DERIVED here, never stored — the equal-weighted sum of the four axes, and
  3570	            # the override wins over it for ranking wherever both exist.
  3571	            score = "-"
  3572	            if _col(r, "rating_pri") is not None:
  3573	                calc = sum(_col(r, c) for c in RATING_COLUMNS[:4])
  3574	                score = str(calc)
  3575	                if _col(r, "rating_ovr") is not None:
  3576	                    score = "%s>%d" % (score, r["rating_ovr"])
  3577	            print("%s  %-7s %-22s #%-3d cre=%-6s calc=%-8s %s %s" % (
  3578	                r["global_id"], gh, (r["section"] or "")[:22], r["position"], cre, score,
  3579	                r["status_marker"] or " ", r["title"][:64]))
  3580	    finally:
  3581	        conn.close()
  3582

  3584	def resolve_issue_number(target):
  3585	    """Resolve a target string or int into a GitHub issue number (int)."""
   125	
   126	### 8. Stay quiet on trivial work
   127	
   128	Most edits are small and reversible. Do not manufacture ceremony for a rename, typo fix, or other
   129	local change.
   130	
   131	## Repo-specific rails
   132	
   133	- **This repo's purpose is to keep a long-horizon marathon under load — and that is a work-selection
   134	  filter, not a slogan.** The harness is only proven by work long enough, parallel enough, and
   135	  failure-prone enough to tax the whole system: worktree isolation, path claims, the driver lock,
   136	  multi-round handoff, escalation, and resume. Short, single-shot tasks land fine but prove nothing.
   137	  Four rules follow, and they are load-bearing:
   138	
   139	  1. **Exactly one long-horizon marathon is in flight at a time.** When one lands, choosing the next
   140	     is a real decision, not a default. It is named in the roadmap ledger's **Immediate next-up**
   141	     (read `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`; the RELEASES DB is the source of truth since the
   142	     `ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases` flip) as the marathon, so an agent arriving cold can tell which item
   143	     is the load and which items are riding alongside it.
   144	  2. **Prefer the marathon-shaped candidate.** *Marathon-shaped* means: decomposable into many items
   145	     with an identical transform, a per-item pass condition a machine can check, and a plausible way
   146	     to break the harness. GH-10 (73 unaudited suites, one mechanical adoption each) is the
   147	     archetype. Picking a non-marathon-shaped item over a marathon-shaped one of comparable value
   148	     needs a stated reason — write it in the ledger entry, not in a commit message.
   149	  3. **Only real work.** Never manufacture a marathon to keep the system busy, and never build a
   150	     synthetic workload that cannot damage anything — a run with no blast radius does not surface
   151	     the failures that matter. If nothing genuinely needed is marathon-shaped right now, **the
   152	     correct state is idle**. Say so plainly and do the smaller work; an idle gap is honest signal,
   153	     a fabricated marathon is noise that costs real tokens.
   154	  4. **The point is the failures.** A marathon that completes cleanly and teaches nothing is a
   155	     weaker result than one that escalates and names a defect. Report what broke; do not smooth it.
   156	
   157	- **The RELEASES DB is two subsystems behind one CLI** (`utils/py/releases_app.py`): the GH-32
   158	  release ledger and the roadmap ledger (`roadmap_items`). Since the `ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases`
   159	  flip (GH-169/GH-238/GH-243) and ROADMAP.md retirement (GH-269), the DB is the roadmap's source of truth in THIS repo: park intake
   160	  with `releases roadmap add` (or `hq park`), and read with `roadmap list` / `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`.
   161	  `roadmap sync` is for legacy-mode repos only and no-ops here — it mirrors markdown and would delete
   162	  `add`-parked rows. Never hand-edit `releases.sql` or `releases.db`. Merge conflicts on the dump have
   163	  a one-command resolver (`utils/releases-merge-resolve.sh`). The whole contract, including what a real
   164	  merge conflict looks like: [RELEASES-DB-FAQS.md](RELEASES-DB-FAQS.md).
   165	
   166	- **The local gate runs at the push boundary; hosted CI independently attests public-repo changes
   167	  (GH-544, XYZ-forge #16).** The private-phase bridge ended when this repository became public on
   168	  2026-08-15. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` now covers `push`/`pull_request` on `main` and `development`;
   169	  the macOS promotion boundary remains restricted to a push on `main`, while the Ubuntu job is an
   170	  advisory portability canary. Local commits are ungated on purpose; pushes are still gated by
     1	---
     2	name: express
     3	description: >
     4	  Hotfix fast lane (GH-267) for an explicit `/express`, "express this hotfix",
     5	  or "express GH-N" request. Carries a critical, risk-bounded fix, registered
     6	  regression suite, releases-ledger updates, born-complete PDDA doc, CHANGELOG,
     7	  landing, and reconciliation in one operator-authorized motion. Requires a
     8	  fresh full task clone branched from origin/development with the canonical
     9	  pre-push gate installed, authenticated gh, and the root releases ledger. The
    10	  driver opens and immediately merges a PR to development so issue closure and
    11	  `wave_reconcile --pr` remain available; "direct" means no human pause, not no
    12	  PR. Refuses shared/stale clones, unsafe Git/Bash/kernel surfaces, oversized or
    13	  multi-subsystem diffs, generated-artifact hand edits, missing/red suites, and
    14	  closed or unresolved issues. Do not use for Costly or one-way-door changes.
    15	---
    16	
    17	# /express — hotfix fast lane through the whole paper trail
    18	
    19	One verb, one motion: the fix, its regression suite, the ledger writes, the
    20	born-complete capture doc, the CHANGELOG entry, the landing, and the
    21	reconciliation — all before the operator's coffee cools. The guardrails are the
    22	skill; the speed is a side effect.
    23	
    24	**Design provenance:** proposed on #259 (comment 5434441831, v2), filed as
    25	#267, built consistently with the jog plan (`PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-259-JOG-SERIAL-QUEUE.md`):
    26	re-use the releases verbs, `wave_reconcile`, the pre-push gate, and `.tick`
    27	events rather than building parallel machinery. Where jog pauses at each
    28	landing boundary by default (orchestrator outer review), express inlines those
    29	same predicates — base branch, diff size, gate receipts — as pre-flight
    30	refusals, because a pause would defeat the reason /express exists. That is the
    31	foundational difference, and it is the only one.
    32	
    33	## Procedure
    34	
    35	Run the driver; it enforces the order. Do not hand-perform steps the driver owns.
    36	
    37	```bash
    38	# 0. From the task clone that already carries the fix (SOP §4 clone, hooks installed):
    39	python3 utils/py/express.py check --issue <N> --suite test/gh<N>-<slug>.sh   # steps 0–4
    40	python3 utils/py/express.py docs  --issue <N> --suite test/gh<N>-<slug>.sh --summary "<one line>"  # step 5
    41	python3 utils/py/express.py ledger --issue <N>                               # step 6
    42	python3 utils/py/express.py land  --issue <N> --suite test/gh<N>-<slug>.sh   # steps 7–11
    43	# or the whole motion at once:
    44	python3 utils/py/express.py run --issue <N> --suite test/gh<N>-<slug>.sh --summary "<one line>"
    45	```
    46	
    47	What each phase asserts (all refusals write `.tick/events/*-express-refused-*.jsonl`):
    48	
    49	0. **Tree of execution** — task branch, HEAD == origin/development, no scratch
    50	   paths, and `bash githooks/install.sh --check` proves the canonical pre-push
    51	   stub (`gate-unwired`; hooks do not travel, GH-549). Express never commits
    52	   over peer work (GH-527). Hand-edits to ledger files or generated views are
    53	   refused; only the driver's verbs may write them.
    54	1. **Bounds** — ≤ 4 core files / ≤ 150 insertions, single subsystem. Only the
    55	   lane's OWN paperwork (`CHANGELOG.md`, `PROJECT/**`) is exempt — operator
    56	   .md edits (README, governance, skills) COUNT (a gateless merge never
    57	   rewrites policy unbounded). Defaults are operator-tunable via
    58	   `--max-files` / `--max-insertions`; the refusals are never optional.
    59	2. **Hard refusals** — frozen twins and shared Bash runtime (GH-308), any
    60	   new/edited `.sh` under `utils/` or `relay-automation/` (GH-551),
    61	   coordination-kernel and containment surfaces (AGENTS: at least Costly).
    62	3. **Issue first** — the tracking issue exists and is OPEN. Closed => the work
    63	   may already be landed; run the preflight probes instead of re-doing it.
    64	4. **Suite** — the fix's regression suite exists AND is registered in
    65	   `validate.sh` TESTS. A hotfix without its suite is a claim, not a fix.
    66	5. **Docs born complete** — capture doc scaffolded in `2-WORKING` with Status,
    67	   Acceptance, Merge evidence, and `## Lessons Learned (For Future Agents)`
    68	   present from birth (the 08-26 reconcile gate refuses promotion otherwise),
    69	   plus the CHANGELOG entry appended in the same motion.
    70	6. **Ledger** — `roadmap add` if the issue is unparked, then `manifest dial-in`
    71	   against the active release (`releases next`) with an express reason. The
    72	   adopted release/leaderboard projections and `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` refresh
    73	   in the same phase and are the only accepted driver outputs.
    74	7. **Gate** — the fix's suite runs green, the tree is RE-SNAPSHOTTED afterwards
    75	   by path and content (`tree-drift`: new paths and changed qualified bytes both
    76	   refuse). Gate identity is re-proven after the suite, staging uses explicit
    77	   pathspecs, and the push never sets `XYZ_SKIP_PREPUSH`.
    78	8. **Land** — one commit of exactly the qualified paths; push; ghost PR into
    79	   `development`; immediate merge. The closeout then switches to clean,
    80	   current `development` (ship/reconcile state never rides the task branch).
    81	9. **Ship with evidence** — `manifest ship --gid <rel> --evidence "<sha>; <suite>
    82	   green; PR #<m> merged"` — post-merge, so the sha and receipts exist when the
    83	   evidence is written. The GH-205 trap (dialed_in while closed) is structurally
    84	   impossible in this order.
    85	10. **Close the issue** — the ghost PR body says `Closes #<N>`, so GitHub
    86	    auto-closes it; the driver verifies and closes explicitly if it did not.
    87	11. **Persist, reconcile cleanly, persist — fail closed.** From `development`,
    88	    ship outputs are committed and pushed first. Only then does
    89	    `wave_reconcile.py --pr <m>` run from the clean tree (offline-manifest
    90	    fallback for foreign-tracker citations); its outputs form a second commit
    91	    and push. Every post-merge fault exits non-zero with an
    92	    `express-reconcile-failed` receipt; success prints only after both boundaries.
    93	
    94	## After the run
    95	
    96	- Confirm the tail: `releases check` clean, `pdda.sh issue-doc-sync` 0 errors,
    97	  capture doc promoted (or, under an OPEN umbrella, correctly left in
    98	  `2-WORKING` with merge evidence — that is success, not failure).
    99	- `standup` reports the weekly express count; a rising counter means the normal
   100	  lane is too slow — fix the lane, don't normalize express.
   101	
   102	## Non-goals
   103	
   104	- No override flag: a refused run routes to the normal fresh-clone PR lane,
   105	  full stop. An `--force` would make every guardrail negotiable.
   106	- No Costly/one-way-door work, ever (see step 2 refusals).
   107	- No true push-to-`development` mode — Phase 2, pending `wave_reconcile
   108	  --commit` (tracked on #267).
    95	(report-only, non-destructive) and never clobbers existing seeds. Do **not** pass
    96	`--force` (it overwrites seed ledgers and startup-doc scaffolds). Pass
    97	`--with-startup-docs` only if the target wants the ROUTER/AGENTS scaffold and
    98	does not already have its own. Re-running later upgrades in place.
    99	
   100	### 5. Check and prompt for `ROUTER.md` updates (GH-353)
   101	
   102	After vendoring or updating XYZ in `<target-repo>`:
   103	
   104	Run the router roadmap audit check:
   105	```bash
   106	python3 "$HARNESS/utils/py/router_audit.py" --check /abs/path/to/target-repo
   107	```
   108	
   109	If `ROUTER DRIFT` is reported:
   110	The LLM MUST inspect the reported mode (or `--json` output) and prompt the user for confirmation before modifying the target repository's `ROUTER.md`:
   111	- **If releases mode is active (`releases.db` or `ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases`):**
   112	  > *"The target repository `<target-repo>` has releases mode enabled (`releases.db`), but its `ROUTER.md` still lists `ROADMAP.md` as active instead of frozen. Would you like me to update `ROUTER.md` to mark `ROADMAP.md` as frozen and reference `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` and `releases.db`?"*
   113	- **If legacy mode is active (no releases DB):**
   114	  > *"The target repository `<target-repo>` is in legacy roadmap mode, but its `ROUTER.md` references releases-mode artifacts (`ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` or frozen `ROADMAP.md`). Would you like me to update `ROUTER.md` to restore active `ROADMAP.md` instructions?"*
   115	
   116	Upon user approval:
   117	```bash
   118	python3 "$HARNESS/utils/py/router_audit.py" --fix /abs/path/to/target-repo
   119	```
   120	
   121	### 6. Verify
   122	
   123	```bash
   124	cd /abs/path/to/target-repo
   125	./.xyz/bin/tick --help >/dev/null && echo "tick OK"        # XYZ runnable
   126	grep -qx '.xyz/' .gitignore && echo ".xyz gitignored"
   127	utils/pdda/pdda.sh run && echo "PDDA runs"                  # only if PDDA installed
   128	```
   129	
   130	Then confirm the registry rows landed (this is the "called home" proof):

./CHANGELOG.md-1849-### Marathon graduation — first real end-to-end fire (GH-27 + WPCC), two concurrent dogfoods
./CHANGELOG.md-1850-The Marathon harness **fired for real for the first time** (every prior dogfood died at substrate-starvation before launch). Two targets built concurrently in isolated git worktrees (separate indexes, no collision), each `swarm-preflight → marathon-drive → codex build → gate → agy review`:
./CHANGELOG.md:1851:- **GH-27 ROADMAP dashboard ✅** — codex built a dependency-free renderer ([utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh), 201 lines) + the generated [ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md](ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md) + [test/roadmap-dashboard.sh](test/roadmap-dashboard.sh); agy **Approved** (3 turns); gate 4/4. Same-repo, on `marathon/gh-27-dashboard`. Completion fix: wired the test into `validate.sh` (codex missed it; agy approved without catching — a dogfood finding) → **`validate.sh` 48/48**.
./CHANGELOG.md-1852-- **WPCC `ts-type-suppression` ✅** — codex built the first slice of WP-Code-Check issue #129 (advisory `.ts`/`.tsx` grep detector for `@ts-ignore`/`@ts-nocheck`/bare `@ts-expect-error`); agy **Approved**; gate passed; the scanner detects **3/3** on the fixture (documented `@ts-expect-error // reason` excluded). Committed to `WP-Code-Check` `marathon-dogfood/ts-type-suppression` (`3e22f97`).
./CHANGELOG.md-1853-- **Findings (the dogfood's real payload):** (1) **containment held on real load** — WPCC v1 escalated when `pattern-library-manager.sh` regenerated an off-allowlist `PATTERN-LIBRARY.md`; the guard reverted it (exit 6). (2) **GH-22 fix validated live** — agy reviewed under worktree isolation on GH-27 and its block was preserved. (3) **New bug → [#29](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/29)**: cross-repo (`--target-root`) builds don't commit the builder's NEW untracked files (`no tracked changes`), so a correct, gate-passing, Approved build lands uncommitted — recovered by hand-commit; `RELAY_WORKTREE_ISOLATION=0` does not fix it.

./utils/py/router_audit.py:10:  - Asserts ## Startup sequence directs to ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md for current work/state/active effort in the directive clause itself (rejecting purpose-free reads like 'Read ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md for deployment instructions; TEAM.md tracks current work', 'not current state', 'historical reference only', 'do not read') and forbids active ROADMAP.md directives in all clauses (supporting valid negations like 'do not use ROADMAP.md', while preserving custom steps and historical reads like 'Read ROADMAP.md only for historical reference').

./ROUTER.md-9-- `AGENTS.md` = behavioral rules, decision quality, reversibility, blast radius, proof
./ROUTER.md-10-- `README.md` = human-facing repo/product overview
./ROUTER.md:11:- `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` = the generated, human-readable view of the roadmap ledger (read this; regenerate with `utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`); the RELEASES DB (`releases.db` via `releases.sql`) is the source of truth (`ROADMAP.md` retired per GH-269)
./ROUTER.md-12-- `CHANGELOG.md` = the end-of-iteration running log (first-class PDDA artifact; governed by `PROJECT/PDDA.md`)
./ROUTER.md-13-- `RELEASES.md` = forward-looking release-planning ledger (first-class PDDA artifact; governed by `PROJECT/PDDA.md`)
--
./ROUTER.md-25-1. Read `ROUTER.md` to understand the repo's operating order and canonical files. -> expect one clear next file, not a repo-wide scavenger hunt.
./ROUTER.md-26-2. Read `AGENTS.md` before making recommendations or edits. -> expect explicit assumptions, a reversibility read on consequential changes, and verified claims only.
./ROUTER.md:27:3. Read `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (or `python3 utils/py/releases_app.py roadmap list`) to find the active effort or parked intake. -> expect links outward to the canonical `PROJECT/**` docs; the roadmap is a pointer ledger, not a plan body. (`ROADMAP.md` is retired — the RELEASES DB is the single source of truth.)
./ROUTER.md-28-4. Read the linked `PROJECT/**` document that owns the work you are touching. -> expect the near-top `## Status` table to tell you what was just completed and what is next.
./ROUTER.md-29-5. If the task touches project docs, read `PROJECT/PDDA.md` and follow the PDDA contract. -> expect `PROJECT/2-WORKING` docs to have frontmatter, the exact status table, and QA gates when phased.
--
./ROUTER.md-149-**Subsystem 2 — the roadmap ledger** (GH-69 shadow → GH-238/GH-243 canonical → GH-269 retired ROADMAP.md): since the
./ROUTER.md-150-`ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases` flip, `roadmap_items` IS the ledger — write rows with
./ROUTER.md:151:`releases roadmap add` (or `hq park`), read with `roadmap list` / `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`.
./ROUTER.md-152-`ROADMAP.md` has been retired and removed. `roadmap sync` exists for legacy-mode repos only and
./ROUTER.md-153-no-ops here by design (it would delete `add`-parked rows). Pinned by `test/gh69-roadmap-shadow.sh`,
--
./ROUTER.md-156-## Routing hints
./ROUTER.md-157-
./ROUTER.md:158:- If the task is about current priorities or active work, start in `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (or `releases roadmap list`), then follow the linked `PROJECT/**` doc.
./ROUTER.md:159:- If the task changes the roadmap ledger, write through the CLI (`releases roadmap add` for intake; `hq park` routes there automatically) and finish by regenerating `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (`utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`) — the push gate refuses a ledger write with a stale dashboard (GH-243).
./ROUTER.md-160-- If the task touches `releases.db`, `releases.sql`, or a merge conflict on either, start in [RELEASES-DB-FAQS.md](RELEASES-DB-FAQS.md); writes go through the CLI, never a hand-edit.
./ROUTER.md-161-- If the task is about fresh GitHub intake or duplicate-prevention, start in the dashboard's queue section (or `releases roadmap list`), then follow the linked `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-*.md` capture doc.
--
./ROUTER.md-169-- If the task is about relay session telemetry, the `focus5float` health feed, or extraction scripts under `utils/telemetry/`, start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-24-RELAY-TELEMETRY-EXTRACTOR.md`.
./ROUTER.md-170-- If the task is about live per-session completion telemetry — the `XYZ.json` log every relay/marathon/swarm session appends to at the harness repo root (schema: `harness`/`sessionId`/`health`/`title`/`description`/`updatedAt`), the shared writer `utils/telemetry/append-xyz-completion.sh`, or the shared health mapping `utils/telemetry/health-lib.sh` — start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-75-XYZ-JSON-COMPLETION-TELEMETRY.md`. `XYZ.json` is local + gitignored (machine-specific).
./ROUTER.md:171:- If the task is about cross-repo HQ tooling (`utils/hq/` — `hq.sh` single-repo actions, `rollup.sh` the Obsidian daily ROADMAP rollup, `marathon-scan.sh` the cross-repo marathon-preflight aggregator, `hq-lib.sh` the shared repo registry), start in `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-27-ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` and `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-158-HQ-MARATHON-SCAN.md`. The two rollups are deliberately separate today (`rollup.sh` → Obsidian, generic; `marathon-scan.sh` → hub repo, preflight-aware) and are not yet bridged — tracked in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-192-HQ-MARATHON-OBSIDIAN-ROLLUP.md`.
./ROUTER.md-172-- If the task is about a proposed roadmap-steward agent, start here, then read `PROJECT/PDDA.md` and its `Proposed roadmap steward extension` section.
./ROUTER.md-173-- If the task is about finding or picking a skill for a job, see `ARCHITECTURE.md` → "Skills Index" for a one-line inventory of every skill in `skills/`.
--

./relay-system/2026-09-02/gh396-plan-review-qwen.md-197-**→ REQUIRES CLARIFICATION IN PLAN**
./relay-system/2026-09-02/gh396-plan-review-qwen.md-198-
./relay-system/2026-09-02/gh396-plan-review-qwen.md:199:`wave_reconcile.py:40-70` (`harness_tool`) returns `$REPO/utils/…` when it exists, else the harness copy. The plan's Phase 2 extracts this as `resolve_tool(rel_path)` with the same repo-first order. A consumer repo that carries its own `utils/py/releases_app.py` or `utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh` — unrelated to the harness, same filename — would silently shadow the harness tool.
./relay-system/2026-09-02/gh396-plan-review-qwen.md-200-
./relay-system/2026-09-02/gh396-plan-review-qwen.md-201-**Is this a real risk for the three consumer repos (rebalanceOS, LTVera-Pandas, aegis-sleuth-slack-bot)?** Low but non-zero. None of them ships `releases_app.py` at their root. However, the shared module's adoption surface is wider than three known consumers, and `test/gh358-wave-reconcile-vendored-paths.sh:128-131` explicitly pins the repo-first behaviour ("a canonical checkout must prefer its own copy") — that test deliberately expects a local `releases_app.py` to win over the harness copy. This is the existing contract for **harness tools** (the five files that live in both places).
--

./relay-system/2026-09-05/gh452-release-tags-plan-qa.md-112-- [Should] S2 — Candidate locator not reproducible as written: `git rev-list -1 --before <ship-date> origin/development` (plan:25). A bare date is parsed by git's approxidate at the **current time of day**: run at ~23:14 on 2026-09-05 it returns `adee7466`/`bea2e548`/`77b2abe1`/`0520cc39` — none of the four tabled SHAs (probe-f). Fix (verified deterministic, two identical runs, probe-n): `git rev-list -1 --before "<ship-date> 23:59:59 -0700" origin/development` reproduces exactly `136eafbc`/`cd0f5bdb`/`713ba6d1`/`17b18b84`. Keep the manifest-confirmation requirement as-is.
./relay-system/2026-09-05/gh452-release-tags-plan-qa.md-113-- [Should] S3 — Carve-out spec (step 2, plan:39) under-defined and overclaiming fail-closed: (a) "whose target commits are already reachable from **the pushed branch**" is undefined for a tag-only push (no branch is pushed) — pin it to: the peeled commit is an ancestor of **origin/development or origin/main** (remote-tracking refs), NOT any side branch, else a commit that landed on a side branch via the docs/tier-2 route or a bypass inherits the suite skip (the DoD's side-branch vector); (b) reachable ≠ gated: `--no-verify` / `XYZ_SKIP_PREPUSH=1` are documented bypasses (pre-push:33–35) and GitHub-side merges never run the local gate, so "(the commits were gated when they landed)" must be restated as an accepted residual risk; (c) add the forced tag-update/re-point arm: non-zero remote SHA on `refs/tags/v*` → full-gate or refuse — "never re-point a published tag" is policy, the hook needs the enforcement arm; (d) step 2's expe

./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md-408-- If the task is about relay session telemetry, the `focus5float` health feed, or extraction scripts under `utils/telemetry/`, start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-24-RELAY-TELEMETRY-EXTRACTOR.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md-409-- If the task is about live per-session completion telemetry — the `XYZ.json` log every relay/marathon/swarm session appends to at the harness repo root (schema: `harness`/`sessionId`/`health`/`title`/`description`/`updatedAt`), the shared writer `utils/telemetry/append-xyz-completion.sh`, or the shared health mapping `utils/telemetry/health-lib.sh` — start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-75-XYZ-JSON-COMPLETION-TELEMETRY.md`. `XYZ.json` is local + gitignored (machine-specific).
./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md:410:- If the task is about cross-repo HQ tooling (`utils/hq/` — `hq.sh` single-repo actions, `rollup.sh` the Obsidian daily ROADMAP rollup, `marathon-scan.sh` the cross-repo marathon-preflight aggregator, `hq-lib.sh` the shared repo registry), start in `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-27-ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` and `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-158-HQ-MARATHON-SCAN.md`. The two rollups are deliberately separate today (`rollup.sh` → Obsidian, generic; `marathon-scan.sh` → hub repo, preflight-aware) and are not yet bridged — tracked in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-192-HQ-MARATHON-OBSIDIAN-ROLLUP.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md-411-- If the task is about a proposed roadmap-steward agent, start here, then read `PROJECT/PDDA.md` and its `Proposed roadmap steward extension` section.
./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md-412-- Issue-first SOP: any change beyond a 2–3 line fix (and every project plan) opens a GitHub issue *first*, then gets a pointer doc named after the issue at `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-<number>-VERY-SHORT-DESC.md` — e.g. `GH-1234-SHOWME-COMMAND.md` — and that capture is parked in the `ROADMAP.md` queue immediately (format + lifecycle owned by `PROJECT/PDDA.md` → "GitHub issue intake"), following the normal `1-INBOX` → `2-WORKING` flow. Genuinely trivial edits (≤2–3 line fixes, typos, path repoints, doc-only one-liners) are exempt and commit directly.

./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md-1151-- **GH-400 · `/10days` capture docs restate a GitHub issue's acceptance criteria instead of copying them — a measured case inverted one, and the marathon delivered the inversion** ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-03 (PR #413, `d06139c`); closed — re-verified before registering** — Step 4 has a model summarise an issue into `PROJECT/2-WORKING/`, and everything downstream (preflight → packet → relay file → builder → reviewer) reads the **summary**, never the issue; nothing compares the two. Independently re-derived from both source repos rather than taken on the report's word: `rebalance-OS` #202 requires the malformed row be *"never silently dropped"*, the capture doc requires asserting *"the actual current behavior (drop the row…)"*, and the delivered test (`3673257f`, reproduced identically by a second run at `7525d047`) adds a function named **`malformed_source_row_is_dropped`** — every quoted string matches byte-for-byte, and the del
./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md-1152-- **GH-381 · `/Releases` rolling release-train planner** 🆕 **captured 2026-07-30** — proposal for a preview-first planning skill that lays out 4–5 upcoming releases, each with four or five explicit iteration slots, and hands one bounded milestone-derived iteration to `/10days` without duplicating the `RELEASES.md` ledger or GitHub issue membership. → [GH-381-RELEASES-ROLLING-TRAIN.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-381-RELEASES-ROLLING-TRAIN.md) · [#381](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/381)
./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md:1153:- **GH-351 · the registered dashboard gate graded its own answer key** ✅ **done (2026-07-30)** — `test/roadmap-dashboard.sh` rendered the dashboard **into the repo** and then `--check`ed the copy it had just written, so its drift assertion could never fail; its own pass string read *"--check passes on the committed artifact"*, which was false by the time it ran. **The renderer was never the defect** — standalone `utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh --check` returned `rc=1` on all three of the day's stale dashboards, so the correct signal existed the whole time and the test overwrote its input before asking. A second, quieter fault: the test **wrote into `$ROOT`**, so `validate.sh` silently un-staled the dashboard mid-run — a suite repairing the evidence it was meant to judge, and leaving the tree dirty. Three stale dashboards reached `development` on 2026-07-30 alone from three different authors (GH-342, #356, #350); it fires on ess
./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md-1154-- **GH-369 · `/10days`' capture-doc lookup hung forever on a valueless `--root`, in a skill built to run unattended** ✅ **done (2026-07-30)** — found reviewing PR #364 before merge; that PR's own GH-344 fix is correct and shipped, these are defects in the argument parser it introduced. `shift 2` with one argument left shifts nothing and returns non-zero, and `find-doc.sh` is deliberately `-e`-exempt, so `$#` never decreased, `$1` stayed `--root`, and the loop re-entered forever — confirmed at **rc=142/SIGALRM** under an `alarm(5)` wrapper. The severity is not the malformed call but the *shape*: `/10days` runs unattended, so this stalls a sweep with no error, no output and no timeout — the absent-signal class of #315/#319/#351, not a wrong answer. Second defect: a bad `$TENDAYS_ROOT` reported `--root not a directory:`, naming a flag never passed and printing an empty path; the resolver now records which of its four sources s
./relay-system/2026-08-17/consult-130916/consult.codex.md-1155-- **GH-354 · concurrent swarms: the driver lock blocks 1 of 3 pairs, and the monitors can't see the one it does** 🟡 **active — Phase 0 discovery complete 2026-07-30; Phase 1 (relay-drive worktree lock) next** — review of [#354](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/354)'s own analysis, which reasoned about `relay-drive.sh`/`relay-turn-lib.sh` from `marathon-drive.sh`'s comments because it lacked the files. ./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-71-
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md:72:- [Blocker] Releases-mode validation is not scoped to the required blocks and can report a stale router as clean. A `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` mention anywhere satisfies `has_dashboard`, while an omitted `ROADMAP.md = ...` role entry skips the frozen check entirely; startup detection only recognizes one exact numbered `Read ROADMAP.md` spelling (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:71-103`). Fix: parse the bounded `## Role split` and `## Startup sequence` sections, require the dashboard and frozen/source-of-truth declarations in Role split, reject active roadmap reads in Startup, and add negative tests for mentions outside those sections, missing role entries, case/format variants, and absent Startup guidance.
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-73-- [Blocker] Legacy-mode validation does not detect the false frozen references required by DoD 3. It only reports a dashboard mention when the dashboard file is absent, never inspects a frozen/legacy `ROADMAP.md` declaration, and therefore treats a legacy repo with a leftover dashboard file—or a frozen declaration alone—as clean (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:104-110`). Fix: reject releases-only dashboard/frozen/source-of-truth declarations based on mode regardless of filesystem leftovers, then test both false-clean cases.
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-74-- [Blocker] `--fix` can return success without producing a clean router and can rewrite custom sections. Releases remediation never creates a missing Role split, fails to append a dashboard at EOF when the section lacks matching entries, and rewrites every matching numbered roadmap-read line globally rather than only Startup (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:132-195`). Legacy remediation likewise deletes/replaces matching lines globally and hard-codes step `3`, losing original numbering/text (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:196-225`). Fix: locate or create bounded sections, mutate only their owned entries while preserving all other bytes, handle section-at-EOF, preserve file metadata during atomic replacement, and re-run `audit_router` before returning success; cover missing/last sections and custom lookalike prose in tests.
--

./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-117-- [Blocker] Mode detection is still a raw substring search, so commented or unrelated assignments such as `# ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases` and `NOT_ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases` incorrectly classify a legacy repo as releases mode (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:24-35`). Fix: parse an uncommented, anchored assignment with permitted whitespace (and surface an unreadable `.pdda-mode` as an audit error rather than silently choosing legacy); add positive whitespace and negative comment/prefix tests.
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-118-- [Blocker] Releases-mode auditing still has false-clean active-roadmap cases. The Role split accepts any one of `frozen`, `legacy`, `releases.db`, or `releases.sql`, so `ROADMAP.md = active; releases.db exists` passes, while Startup rejects only an exactly numbered `Read ROADMAP.md` line and therefore misses bullets, unnumbered directives, and other active wording (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:136-179`). Fix: require both an explicit frozen/legacy declaration and releases-DB source-of-truth guidance in the ROADMAP role entry, and reject active ROADMAP directives throughout the bounded Startup block regardless of list marker/backticks/case; add the promised format/semantic-variant tests.
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md:119:- [Blocker] Legacy auditing does not enforce its own active-roadmap contract and still misses false-frozen Startup references. Missing Role split/Startup sections are accepted, and Startup checks only for `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`, so a Startup line declaring `ROADMAP.md` frozen passes (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:184-219`). Remediation correspondingly never creates missing legacy sections or inserts an active ROADMAP step, yet the weak post-audit can certify that incomplete result (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:337-367`, `.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:392-395`). Fix: require both bounded sections in legacy mode, require an active `ROADMAP.md` role and Startup directive, reject frozen/legacy/releases-source declarations in both, and create/repair missing sections under `--fix`; cover each false-clean and remediation path.
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-120-- [Should] “Preserves custom sections byte-for-byte” is not established: the fixer splits the entire document with `splitlines()` and reconstructs every section with `"\n".join(...)`, which normalizes non-LF line endings outside the owned blocks (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:236-237`, `.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:369-379`). Fix: splice only the bounded section ranges while retaining untouched source slices and their line endings, or narrow the stated guarantee and add an exact before/after assertion for custom bytes; the current test checks only two retained substrings (`test/gh353-vendored-router-audit.sh:163-168`).
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-121-- [Should] The expanded test is 12 scenarios / 23 assertions, not 23 comprehensive cases, and it omits the mode-parser, format-variant, legacy-missing-section, and legacy-Startup-frozen regressions above (`test/gh353-vendored-router-audit.sh:4-16`, `test/gh353-vendored-router-audit.sh:49-270`). ./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-251-- [Blocker] Legacy Role split auditing still allows a contradictory frozen declaration when any separate line satisfies the active-role predicate. The block-wide check rejects only releases tokens, while `frozen`/`legacy` are considered only inside the individual line selected by `is_legacy_active_role_line`; therefore a canonical active `ROADMAP.md` entry plus another `ROADMAP.md is frozen legacy` line passes clean (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:112-122`, `.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:229-242`). The legacy fixer also leaves that second line untouched because it removes only releases-token lines and rewrites only `- ROADMAP.md =` declarations (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:379-397`). Fix: reject frozen/legacy ROADMAP semantics anywhere in the bounded Role split, remediate every such owned reference, and add a two-line contradictory-role audit/fix regression.
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md:252:- [Blocker] Releases-mode auditing does not require affirmative dashboard guidance. A Role split declaration such as ``ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md = obsolete; do not read`` satisfies `has_dashboard`, and a Startup line saying `Do not read ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` satisfies `has_dashboard_startup`; neither predicate evaluates polarity or active/current-work semantics (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:174-186`, `.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:204-215`). Fix: require a non-negated dashboard/release-ledger source-of-truth declaration in Role split and a non-negated current-work directive in Startup, then cover obsolete/do-not-read false-clean cases and their remediation.
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-253-- [Blocker] The Round 4 polarity finding remains unresolved because Startup classification still returns false on the first whole-line frozen/do-not-read phrase before examining a later active clause. For example, `ROADMAP.md is frozen for historical reference; nevertheless use ROADMAP.md for current work` is certified non-active at the early return despite the explicit current-work directive (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:87-107`). Releases Role split detection is likewise a narrow phrase list, so `ROADMAP.md = active; frozen legacy; releases.db is source of truth` omits every listed contradictory phrase and passes (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:191-198`). Fix: evaluate each ROADMAP-bearing clause and its polarity, reject any affirmative active/current/pointer-ledger claim even alongside frozen text, and add these exact contradictory regressions for both audit and `--fix`.
./relay-system/2026-08-31/gh353-router-audit-qa.md-254-- [Should] Legacy Startup's advertised Markdown-link form cannot satisfy the positive directive regex because the pattern places a word boundary after the link's closing `)`, a non-word character; ordinary following whitespace or end-of-line therefore has no boundary (`.relay-artifacts/router_audit.py:256-260`). Fix: terminate the link alternative without the outer `\b` (or parse the target separately) and add a clean `Read [the roadmap](ROADMAP.md) ...` legacy fixture.

./PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-215-RECONCILER-VENDOR-PATHS.md-44-## Reproduction
./PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-215-RECONCILER-VENDOR-PATHS.md-45-1. In a repo with `.xyz/` vendored, run `python3 .xyz/utils/py/wave_reconcile.py --pr <N> --dry-run`.
./PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-215-RECONCILER-VENDOR-PATHS.md:46:2. `run_subprocesses()` builds commands as `python3 utils/py/releases_app.py ...`, `python3 utils/timeline/export_timeline.py --preview`, `bash utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`, `bash utils/marathon-plan.sh` (with `cwd=repo_root`); `run_validation_gate()` builds `bash utils/pdda-local-checks.sh` / `bash utils/pdda/pdda.sh`. None of these paths exist at `repo_root/utils/...` — only at `repo_root/.xyz/utils/...`. Dies at the first subprocess call.
./PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-215-RECONCILER-VENDOR-PATHS.md:47:3. After locally prefixing all six occurrences with `.xyz/`: reaches `bash .xyz/utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`, which itself derives its root via `HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"` then `ROOT="$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)"` — correct only if the script lives at `repo_root/utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`. Vendored one level deeper, `ROOT` resolves to `repo_root/.xyz` instead of `repo_root`, so it crashes with `ENOENT: .../.xyz/ROADMAP.md`.
./PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-215-RECONCILER-VENDOR-PATHS.md-48-
./PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-215-RECONCILER-VENDOR-PATHS.md-49-**Expected:** both resolve correctly against the true repo root in a vendored install.
--

./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md-1153-3. **[#59](https://github.com/HiQS-Suite/XYZ-forge/issues/59) — find out why hosted CI fires on nothing**, then narrow triggers to push/merge on `development` + `main` and wire the required check behind `main`'s new branch protection. **The "fires on nothing" half RESOLVED ITSELF at 17:09 UTC on 2026-08-21** — `pull_request` (run 32506672733) and `push` on `development` (run 32506697196) both fired normally, with no change to the workflow's triggers, after four days and three check-less merges (#116, #121, #122). Cause not established; it changed between 16:45 and 17:09 UTC, and the audit log needs `admin:org` — **if you flipped a setting in that window, record it on the issue.** Seven hypotheses were ruled out with evidence during the outage (billing was the leading theory and was wrong). **Still open:** narrowing the triggers and wiring the required check behind `main`'s branch protection — and note that bot
./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md:1154:4. **[#123](https://github.com/HiQS-Suite/XYZ-forge/issues/123) — clear the Linux portability canary**: 7 failing assertions across 5 suites, found by the FIRST end-to-end run of that job (2026-08-21, run 32504570094). It had been dying at step 1 on a missing shebang and reporting the run green since the 2026-08-17 re-arm — advisory jobs make their own failures invisible. Two of the seven are new code from #116 (`utils/leaderboard.sh` ordering, `roadmap sync` dry-run), so they are drift we shipped, not drift we inherited. Per #232, reproduce in a container before calling any of them environment noise. rated 70/55/45/65.

./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md-2736-    44	## Reproduction
./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md-2737-    45	1. In a repo with `.xyz/` vendored, run `python3 .xyz/utils/py/wave_reconcile.py --pr <N> --dry-run`.
./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md:2738:    46	2. `run_subprocesses()` builds commands as `python3 utils/py/releases_app.py ...`, `python3 utils/timeline/export_timeline.py --preview`, `bash utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`, `bash utils/marathon-plan.sh` (with `cwd=repo_root`); `run_validation_gate()` builds `bash utils/pdda-local-checks.sh` / `bash utils/pdda/pdda.sh`. None of these paths exist at `repo_root/utils/...` — only at `repo_root/.xyz/utils/...`. Dies at the first subprocess call.
./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md:2739:    47	3. After locally prefixing all six occurrences with `.xyz/`: reaches `bash .xyz/utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`, which itself derives its root via `HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"` then `ROOT="$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)"` — correct only if the script lives at `repo_root/utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`. Vendored one level deeper, `ROOT` resolves to `repo_root/.xyz` instead of `repo_root`, so it crashes with `ENOENT: .../.xyz/ROADMAP.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md-2740-    48	
./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md-2741-    49	**Expected:** both resolve correctly against the true repo root in a vendored install.
--

./relay-system/2026-08-25/build-sequence-223459/build-sequence.codex.md-3135-- [Should] Keep version numbers; do not globally renumber or retarget merely for numeric order. Cargo explicitly records that its target date—not its `0.9.0` number—sets sequence (`RELEASES.md:227`), and Sundown’s earlier renumber demonstrates the historical churn cost (`RELEASES.md:213`). Retarget only when evidence forces a date slip, most immediately Linux-RC if the expanded green-run work cannot meet 2026-09-05.

./relay-system/2026-09-06/needle-oracle-140920/needle-oracle.codex.md:206:- If the task is about current priorities or active work, start in `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (or `releases roadmap list`), then follow the linked `PROJECT/**` doc.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/needle-oracle-140920/needle-oracle.codex.md:207:- If the task changes the roadmap ledger, write through the CLI (`releases roadmap add` for intake; `hq park` routes there automatically) and finish by regenerating `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (`utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`) — the push gate refuses a ledger write with a stale dashboard (GH-243).

./relay-system/2026-09-06/needle-oracle-140920/needle-oracle.codex.md-209-- If the task is about fresh GitHub intake or duplicate-prevention, start in the dashboard's queue section (or `releases roadmap list`), then follow the linked `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-*.md` capture doc.

./relay-system/2026-09-06/needle-oracle-140920/needle-oracle.codex.md-218-- If the task is about live per-session completion telemetry — the `XYZ.json` log every relay/marathon/swarm session appends to at the harness repo root (schema: `harness`/`sessionId`/`health`/`title`/`description`/`updatedAt`), the shared writer `utils/telemetry/append-xyz-completion.sh`, or the shared health mapping `utils/telemetry/health-lib.sh` — start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-75-XYZ-JSON-COMPLETION-TELEMETRY.md`. `XYZ.json` is local + gitignored (machine-specific).
./relay-system/2026-09-06/needle-oracle-140920/needle-oracle.codex.md:219:- If the task is about cross-repo HQ tooling (`utils/hq/` — `hq.sh` single-repo actions, `rollup.sh` the Obsidian daily ROADMAP rollup, `marathon-scan.sh` the cross-repo marathon-preflight aggregator, `hq-lib.sh` the shared repo registry), start in `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-27-ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` and `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-158-HQ-MARATHON-SCAN.md`. The two rollups are deliberately separate today (`rollup.sh` → Obsidian, generic; `marathon-scan.sh` → hub repo, preflight-aware) and are not yet bridged — tracked in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-192-HQ-MARATHON-OBSIDIAN-ROLLUP.md`.

./relay-system/2026-09-06/needle-oracle-140920/needle-oracle.codex.md-221-- If the task is about finding or picking a skill for a job, see `ARCHITECTURE.md` → "Skills Index" for a one-line inventory of every skill in `skills/`.

./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md:475:- If the task is about current priorities or active work, start in `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (or `releases roadmap list`), then follow the linked `PROJECT/**` doc.
./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md:476:- If the task changes the roadmap ledger, write through the CLI (`releases roadmap add` for intake; `hq park` routes there automatically) and finish by regenerating `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (`utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh`) — the push gate refuses a ledger write with a stale dashboard (GH-243).
./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md-477-- If the task touches `releases.db`, `releases.sql`, or a merge conflict on either, start in [RELEASES-DB-FAQS.md](RELEASES-DB-FAQS.md); writes go through the CLI, never a hand-edit.
./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md-478-- If the task is about fresh GitHub intake or duplicate-prevention, start in the dashboard's queue section (or `releases roadmap list`), then follow the linked `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-*.md` capture doc.
--
./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md-486-- If the task is about relay session telemetry, the `focus5float` health feed, or extraction scripts under `utils/telemetry/`, start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-24-RELAY-TELEMETRY-EXTRACTOR.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md-487-- If the task is about live per-session completion telemetry — the `XYZ.json` log every relay/marathon/swarm session appends to at the harness repo root (schema: `harness`/`sessionId`/`health`/`title`/`description`/`updatedAt`), the shared writer `utils/telemetry/append-xyz-completion.sh`, or the shared health mapping `utils/telemetry/health-lib.sh` — start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-75-XYZ-JSON-COMPLETION-TELEMETRY.md`. `XYZ.json` is local + gitignored (machine-specific).
./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md:488:- If the task is about cross-repo HQ tooling (`utils/hq/` — `hq.sh` single-repo actions, `rollup.sh` the Obsidian daily ROADMAP rollup, `marathon-scan.sh` the cross-repo marathon-preflight aggregator, `hq-lib.sh` the shared repo registry), start in `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-27-ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` and `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-158-HQ-MARATHON-SCAN.md`. The two rollups are deliberately separate today (`rollup.sh` → Obsidian, generic; `marathon-scan.sh` → hub repo, preflight-aware) and are not yet bridged — tracked in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-192-HQ-MARATHON-OBSIDIAN-ROLLUP.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md-489-- If the task is about a proposed roadmap-steward agent, start here, then read `PROJECT/PDDA.md` and its `Proposed roadmap steward extension` section.
./relay-system/2026-08-25/canary-root-euid-203501/canary-root-euid.codex.md-490-- If the task is about finding or picking a skill for a job, see `ARCHITECTURE.md` → "Skills Index" for a one-line inventory of every skill in `skills/`.
--

Given this phase can `git push`/create/merge a PR — materially higher stakes than Phases 0-2's prompt/config files — the contract required `--dry-run` as a first-class mode and mandated the test suite stub `git`/`gh` (never touch this repo's real remote or GitHub during the build/test turn); verified both directly before closing out, not just trusted the gate. `marathon-closeout.sh`: stage+commit+push → `gh pr create` → `gh pr checks` (red → exit 4, halt before merge) → `gh pr view --json mergeable` (not `MERGEABLE` → exit 4, halt) → `gh pr merge` → switch to `development` → `git pull --ff-only`; command failures normalize to exit 3, argument/precondition errors to exit 2

./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-allowlist-173919/gh243-marathon-allowlist.agy.md:8:You are correct that `marathons` and `issue_refs` do not reach `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`. Tracing `cmd_roadmap_list` (`utils/py/releases_app.py:3545-3581`) shows it performs a flat `SELECT * FROM roadmap_items` with no joins or subqueries. Furthermore, `issue_url` is a plain `TEXT` column (`releases_app.py:605`), not a foreign key, making `issue_refs` completely separable from `marathons`. Finally, the guard protects nothing else but `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (`dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:108-109`).

./relay-system/2026-08-18/gh28-releases-discipline-090759/gh28-releases-discipline.codex.md-437-- If the task is about relay session telemetry, the `focus5float` health feed, or extraction scripts under `utils/telemetry/`, start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-24-RELAY-TELEMETRY-EXTRACTOR.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-18/gh28-releases-discipline-090759/gh28-releases-discipline.codex.md-438-- If the task is about live per-session completion telemetry — the `XYZ.json` log every relay/marathon/swarm session appends to at the harness repo root (schema: `harness`/`sessionId`/`health`/`title`/`description`/`updatedAt`), the shared writer `utils/telemetry/append-xyz-completion.sh`, or the shared health mapping `utils/telemetry/health-lib.sh` — start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-75-XYZ-JSON-COMPLETION-TELEMETRY.md`. `XYZ.json` is local + gitignored (machine-specific).
./relay-system/2026-08-18/gh28-releases-discipline-090759/gh28-releases-discipline.codex.md:439:- If the task is about cross-repo HQ tooling (`utils/hq/` — `hq.sh` single-repo actions, `rollup.sh` the Obsidian daily ROADMAP rollup, `marathon-scan.sh` the cross-repo marathon-preflight aggregator, `hq-lib.sh` the shared repo registry), start in `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-27-ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` and `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-158-HQ-MARATHON-SCAN.md`. The two rollups are deliberately separate today (`rollup.sh` → Obsidian, generic; `marathon-scan.sh` → hub repo, preflight-aware) and are not yet bridged — tracked in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-192-HQ-MARATHON-OBSIDIAN-ROLLUP.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-18/gh28-releases-discipline-090759/gh28-releases-discipline.codex.md-440-- If the task is about a proposed roadmap-steward agent, start here, then read `PROJECT/PDDA.md` and its `Proposed roadmap steward extension` section.
./relay-system/2026-08-18/gh28-releases-discipline-090759/gh28-releases-discipline.codex.md-441-- Issue-first SOP: any change beyond a 2–3 line fix (and every project plan) opens a GitHub issue *first*, then gets a pointer doc named after the issue at `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-<number>-VERY-SHORT-DESC.md` — e.g. `GH-1234-SHOWME-COMMAND.md` — and that capture is parked in the `ROADMAP.md` queue immediately (format + lifecycle owned by `PROJECT/PDDA.md` → "GitHub issue intake"), following the normal `1-INBOX` → `2-WORKING` flow. Genuinely trivial edits (≤2–3 line fixes, typos, path repoints, doc-only one-liners) are exempt and commit directly.
--

./relay-system/2026-08-23/salvage-location-214521/salvage-location.codex.md-1332-./CHANGELOG.md:590:- **The Litmus wave-1 doc sweep dropped from three docs to two, and the ROADMAP's GH-417 entry turned out to be describing someone else's work.** `GH-343` and `GH-418` moved to `3-COMPLETED` (both issues CLOSED). **`GH-425` was pulled back out**: it shipped, closed on per-criterion verification, and was reopened the same day when salvage triage from the abandoned `agy/gh375-385-438-wip` worktree (#458) found a **second call site the fix never reached** — `marathon_drive.py`'s `--log-github` handler resolves `repo` and `issue` independently and compares no slug at all. Sweeping it would have been a false completion claim.
