./relay-system/2026-08-18/merge-order-v2-230654/merge-order-v2.codex.md-248-- Issue-first SOP: any change beyond a 2–3 line fix (and every project plan) opens a GitHub issue *first*, then gets a pointer doc named after the issue at `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-<number>-VERY-SHORT-DESC.md` — e.g. `GH-1234-SHOWME-COMMAND.md` — and that capture is parked in the `ROADMAP.md` queue immediately (format + lifecycle owned by `PROJECT/PDDA.md` → "GitHub issue intake"), following the normal `1-INBOX` → `2-WORKING` flow. Genuinely trivial edits (≤2–3 line fixes, typos, path repoints, doc-only one-liners) are exempt and commit directly.

./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2373-
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md:2374:**Approve with changes.** The two new allowlist arms are safe: neither `marathons` nor `issue_refs` can affect `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`. Do not invert the check to a `roadmap_items` denylist. However, update the now-false jog-specific naming/comments and treat this as a narrow repair—not the durable classifier design.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2375-
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2376-## FINDINGS
--
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2382-- **[Pass] The one-database observation is insufficient by itself, but the static trace supplies the missing proof.** A falsifying fixture would put the same issue URL into both `roadmap_items.issue_url` and `issue_refs.url`, capture `roadmap list --json`, run `marathon add`, and compare the JSON before/after. The outputs must be byte-identical. The code already proves why: the read at lines 3553–3559 never consults `issue_refs`.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2383-
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md:2384:- **[Pass] This guard protects only `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`.** Its changed-path classifier recognizes only the ledger artifacts and that dashboard ([githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:104](githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:104)). Passing it continues into the rest of the pre-push gate; it does not skip the test suite ([githooks/pre-push:83](githooks/pre-push:83), [githooks/pre-push:93](githooks/pre-push:93)). Other consumers of `roadmap list --json` are outside this guard’s contract.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2385-
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2386-- **[Should] Update the classifier’s description and variable name.** After adding marathon support, the claim that the permitted set is exactly the jog fan-out and the name `non_jog_data` become false ([githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:142](githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:142), [githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:147](githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:147)). Rename it to something like `unprojected_data_only`/`projected_or_unknown_data`, and document both allowed fan-outs.
--
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2390-- **[Pass] ATTACK 2: the fail-closed/fail-open framing is correct.** The current catch-all refuses every unknown inserted table ([githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:149](githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:149)); a `roadmap_items`-only denylist would silently accept a future projected table. Two false refusals are meaningful operator-friction evidence, but they do not turn a fail-closed classifier into a fail-open one. They show that the abstraction is becoming maintenance-heavy.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2391-
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md:2392:- **[Should] The durable third option is renderer-owned projection validity, not asking the app for a table allowlist.** The renderer already knows exactly when it drops rows and emits a warning ([utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:175](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:175), [utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:199](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:199), [utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:217](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:217)), but `--check` still exits zero when the resulting file matches ([utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:251](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:251)). Give dropped rows a machine-readable distinct result, then let the guard refuse that result and allow a clean no-diff render regardless of which ledger tables changed. That directly tests the hazardous condition and eliminates both allowlist and denylist drift. File this as follow-up; it need not block the narrow fix.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2393-
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2394-- **[Nit] The literal `INSERT INTO` grammar remains acceptable for this patch.** `_emit` canonically generates exactly that form ([utils/py/releases_app.py:960](utils/py/releases_app.py:960)). A future dump-grammar change would generally create a false refusal, not silently weaken the check. The growing list is an architectural smell, but not a safety defect in these two arms.
--

Passing it continues into the rest of the pre-push gate; it does not skip the test suite ([githooks/pre-push:83](githooks/pre-push:83), [githooks/pre-push:93](githooks/pre-push:93)). ./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2419-- **[Pass] ATTACK 2: the fail-closed/fail-open framing is correct.** The current catch-all refuses every unknown inserted table ([githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:149](githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:149)); a `roadmap_items`-only denylist would silently accept a future projected table. Two false refusals are meaningful operator-friction evidence, but they do not turn a fail-closed classifier into a fail-open one. They show that the abstraction is becoming maintenance-heavy.
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md-2420-
./relay-system/2026-09-06/gh243-marathon-codex-retry-174442/gh243-marathon-codex-retry.codex.md:2421:- **[Should] The durable third option is renderer-owned projection validity, not asking the app for a table allowlist.** The renderer already knows exactly when it drops rows and emits a warning ([utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:175](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:175), [utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:199](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:199), [utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:217](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:217)), but `--check` still exits zero when the resulting file matches ([utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:251](utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:251)). Give dropped rows a machine-readable distinct result, then let the guard refuse that result and allow a clean no-diff render regardless of which ledger tables changed. That directly tests the hazardous condition and eliminates both allowlist and denylist drift. File this as follow-up; it need not block the narrow fix.

./relay-system/2026-08-18/merge-order-230056/merge-order.codex.md-511-- If the task is about relay session telemetry, the `focus5float` health feed, or extraction scripts under `utils/telemetry/`, start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-24-RELAY-TELEMETRY-EXTRACTOR.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-18/merge-order-230056/merge-order.codex.md-512-- If the task is about live per-session completion telemetry — the `XYZ.json` log every relay/marathon/swarm session appends to at the harness repo root (schema: `harness`/`sessionId`/`health`/`title`/`description`/`updatedAt`), the shared writer `utils/telemetry/append-xyz-completion.sh`, or the shared health mapping `utils/telemetry/health-lib.sh` — start in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-75-XYZ-JSON-COMPLETION-TELEMETRY.md`. `XYZ.json` is local + gitignored (machine-specific).
./relay-system/2026-08-18/merge-order-230056/merge-order.codex.md:513:- If the task is about cross-repo HQ tooling (`utils/hq/` — `hq.sh` single-repo actions, `rollup.sh` the Obsidian daily ROADMAP rollup, `marathon-scan.sh` the cross-repo marathon-preflight aggregator, `hq-lib.sh` the shared repo registry), start in `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-27-ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` and `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-158-HQ-MARATHON-SCAN.md`. The two rollups are deliberately separate today (`rollup.sh` → Obsidian, generic; `marathon-scan.sh` → hub repo, preflight-aware) and are not yet bridged — tracked in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-192-HQ-MARATHON-OBSIDIAN-ROLLUP.md`.
./relay-system/2026-08-18/merge-order-230056/merge-order.codex.md-514-- If the task is about a proposed roadmap-steward agent, start here, then read `PROJECT/PDDA.md` and its `Proposed roadmap steward extension` section.
./relay-system/2026-08-18/merge-order-230056/merge-order.codex.md-515-- Issue-first SOP: any change beyond a 2–3 line fix (and every project plan) opens a GitHub issue *first*, then gets a pointer doc named after the issue at `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-<number>-VERY-SHORT-DESC.md` — e.g. `GH-1234-SHOWME-COMMAND.md` — and that capture is parked in the `ROADMAP.md` queue immediately (format + lifecycle owned by `PROJECT/PDDA.md` → "GitHub issue intake"), following the normal `1-INBOX` → `2-WORKING` flow. Genuinely trivial edits (≤2–3 line fixes, typos, path repoints, doc-only one-liners) are exempt and commit directly.
--

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# test/roadmap-dashboard.sh — regression lock for utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh (GH-27).
     3	#
     4	# GH-351: this file used to render the dashboard INTO THE REPO and then --check the copy it had
     5	# just written, so the drift assertion could never fail. Three stale dashboards reached
     6	# `development` under a green gate on 2026-07-30 alone, from three different authors. Two rules
     7	# now hold, and the ordering of the cases below is load-bearing:
     8	#
     9	#   1. --check runs FIRST, against the COMMITTED artifact, before anything in this file writes.
    10	#   2. Nothing here ever writes $ROOT/ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md. Write-mode is exercised against a
    11	#      temp path via ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_OUTPUT, which the renderer already supports.
    12	#
    13	# Case 4 is the one that keeps this honest: it proves --check ACTUALLY FAILS on a drifted
    14	# artifact. Without it this file would just be a differently-shaped version of the same bug —
    15	# an assertion that cannot distinguish the bug from the fix.
    16	
    17	set -uo pipefail
    18	
    19	HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
    20	ROOT="$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)"
    21	RENDERER="$ROOT/utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh"
    22	ARTIFACT="$ROOT/ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md"
    23	SOURCE="$ROOT/ROADMAP.md"
    24	
    25	PASS=0
    26	FAIL=0
    27	SKIP=0
    28	pass() { echo "  PASS: $*"; PASS=$((PASS + 1)); }
    29	fail() { echo "  FAIL: $*" >&2; FAIL=$((FAIL + 1)); }
    30	skip() { echo "  SKIP: $*"; SKIP=$((SKIP + 1)); }
    31	
    32	echo "== test: roadmap-dashboard =="
    33	
    34	# GH-110 P2b: ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md is a repo-root artifact that does not travel with a vendored
    35	# install (VENDOR_DIRS mirrors relay-automation/bin/src/utils/test/skills only). In a vendored copy
    36	# there is nothing here to render/check, so skip the whole ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md-dependent case
    37	# rather than dangle-fail — mirrors the guard already landed in test/oracle-guard.sh.
    38	if [ -e "$ARTIFACT" ]; then
    39	
    40	TMP_ROOT="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/roadmap-dashboard-test.XXXXXX")"
    41	trap 'rm -rf "$TMP_ROOT"' EXIT
    42	
    43	# Fingerprint the committed artifact up front. Case 7 re-checks it at the end to prove this file
    44	# left the working tree alone — the side effect that made the old version destructive as well as
    45	# tautological.
    46	artifact_sum_before="$(cksum < "$ARTIFACT")"
    47	
    48	# --- Case 1: THE GATE. --check against the committed artifact, before anything writes. --------
    49	out="$(bash "$RENDERER" --check 2>&1)"; rc=$?
    50	if [[ $rc -eq 0 ]]; then
    51	  pass "--check passes on the COMMITTED artifact (nothing regenerated first)"
    52	else
    53	  fail "committed ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md is stale — regenerate it with 'bash utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh'"$'\n'"$out"
    54	fi
    55	
    56	# --- Case 2: write-mode still works, redirected away from the repo -----------------------------
    57	tmp_out="$TMP_ROOT/written.md"
    58	out="$(ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_OUTPUT="$tmp_out" bash "$RENDERER" 2>&1)"; rc=$?
    59	if [[ $rc -eq 0 && -s "$tmp_out" ]]; then
    60	  pass "renderer writes a dashboard (to a temp path, never \$ROOT)"
    61	else
    62	  fail "renderer exited $rc or wrote nothing — $out"
    63	fi
    64	
    65	# --- Case 3: a fresh render of the current ROADMAP equals the committed artifact ---------------
    66	# Same property as case 1, reached independently: byte-compare rather than trusting --check's rc.
    67	if cmp -s "$tmp_out" "$ARTIFACT"; then
    68	  pass "a fresh render is byte-identical to the committed artifact"
    69	else
    70	  fail "fresh render differs from the committed artifact:"$'\n'"$(diff -u "$ARTIFACT" "$tmp_out" | head -20)"
    71	fi
    72	
    73	# --- Case 4: THE MUTATION PROOF. --check must actually FAIL on drift. -------------------------
    74	# Without this, cases 1 and 3 are unfalsifiable and this file repeats GH-351 in a new shape.
    75	drift_art="$TMP_ROOT/drifted.md"
    76	cp "$ARTIFACT" "$drift_art"
    77	printf '\n| DRIFT-CANARY | injected-by-test | GH-351 |\n' >> "$drift_art"
    78	out="$(ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_OUTPUT="$drift_art" bash "$RENDERER" --check 2>&1)"; rc=$?
    79	if [[ $rc -ne 0 ]]; then
    80	  pass "--check FAILS on a drifted artifact (rc=$rc) — the assertion is falsifiable"
    81	else
    82	  fail "--check passed a deliberately drifted artifact — GH-351 has regressed in a new shape"
    83	fi
    84	
    85	# --- Case 5: --check fails on a MISSING artifact, rather than passing vacuously ----------------
    86	out="$(ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_OUTPUT="$TMP_ROOT/does-not-exist.md" bash "$RENDERER" --check 2>&1)"; rc=$?
    87	if [[ $rc -ne 0 ]]; then
    88	  pass "--check fails on a missing artifact (rc=$rc)"
    89	else
    90	  fail "--check passed with no artifact present — a vacuous green"
    91	fi
    92	
    93	# --- Case 6: drift in the SOURCE is detected, not just in the artifact -------------------------
    94	# The real-world failure is always this direction: ROADMAP.md moves, the dashboard does not.
    95	drift_src="$TMP_ROOT/ROADMAP-changed.md"
    96	sed 's/^### Queue \/ parked intake$/### Queue \/ parked intake\n- **GH-000 · canary entry injected by test\*\* 🆕 — not a real item./' \
    97	  "$SOURCE" > "$drift_src"
    98	if cmp -s "$drift_src" "$SOURCE"; then
    99	  skip "case 6: could not inject a source canary (ROADMAP.md layout changed)"
   100	else
   101	  out="$(ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_SOURCE="$drift_src" ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_OUTPUT="$ARTIFACT" bash "$RENDERER" --check 2>&1)"; rc=$?
   102	  if [[ $rc -ne 0 ]]; then
   103	    pass "--check FAILS when ROADMAP.md gains an item the dashboard lacks (the real-world case)"
   104	  else
   105	    fail "--check passed while the source had an extra entry — drift is undetectable"
   106	  fi
   107	fi
   108	
   109	# --- Case 7: this test did not mutate the working tree ----------------------------------------
   110	# The old version regenerated $ROOT/ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md as a side effect, so `validate.sh` could
   111	# silently un-stale a dashboard mid-run and hide the very drift it was meant to catch.
   112	if [ "$(cksum < "$ARTIFACT")" = "$artifact_sum_before" ]; then
   113	  pass "committed artifact is byte-unchanged — this test writes nothing into \$ROOT"
   114	else
   115	  fail "this test MUTATED \$ROOT/ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md — the GH-351 side effect is back"
   116	fi
   117	
   118	# --- Case 8: the do-not-edit banner ------------------------------------------------------------
   119	if grep -qx '<!-- GENERATED by utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh — DO NOT EDIT; edit ROADMAP.md -->' "$ARTIFACT"; then
   120	  pass "artifact carries the do-not-edit banner"
   121	else
   122	  fail "artifact is missing the generated banner"
   123	fi
   124	
   125	# --- Case 9: section counts match ROADMAP.md ---------------------------------------------------
   126	# GH-243: in a releases-mode repo the dashboard renders from the RELEASES DB and ROADMAP.md is
   127	# frozen legacy — the DB legitimately gains rows the frozen file never will, so text-parity is
   128	# no longer an invariant. Cases 1/3/4/6 already pin artifact-vs-live-source fidelity via --check
   129	# (which regenerates from the real source, DB included); this case guards legacy repos only.
   130	if grep -q "ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases" "$ROOT/.pdda-mode" 2>/dev/null; then
   131	  pass "case 9 skipped: releases-mode — dashboard parity is against the DB (covered by --check), not the frozen ROADMAP.md"
   132	else
   133	counts_out="$(SOURCE_PATH="$SOURCE" ARTIFACT_PATH="$ARTIFACT" node <<'NODE' 2>&1
   134	const fs = require("fs");
   135	
     1	#!/bin/bash
     2	# test/gh243-dashboard-staleness-guard.sh — the GH-243 push guard: a roadmap-ledger write without a
     3	# dashboard regeneration is refused, releases-mode only, and only when a real range exists.
     4	set -eu
     5	source test/_setup.sh "GH-243" || { echo "setup failed"; exit 1; }
     6	
     7	root="$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)"
     8	GUARD="$root/githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh"
     9	
    10	# Fixture: a releases-mode repo with a base commit, then a ledger-only commit on top.
    11	R="$WORK/fixture"
    12	mkdir -p "$R"
    13	cd "$R"
    14	git init -q .
    15	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q --allow-empty -m base
    16	echo "ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases" > .pdda-mode
    17	echo "-- dump v1" > releases.sql
    18	echo "dash v1" > ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md
    19	git add .pdda-mode releases.sql ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md
    20	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q -m "v1"
    21	BASE="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
    22	
    23	echo "-- dump v2" > releases.sql
    24	git add releases.sql
    25	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q -m "ledger write, no dashboard"
    26	LEDGER_ONLY="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
    27	cd "$root"
    28	
    29	# 1. Ledger write without dashboard -> refuse (exit 1) with the regen instruction.
    30	rc=0; out="$(bash "$GUARD" "$R" "$LEDGER_ONLY" "$BASE" 2>&1)" || rc=$?
    31	[ "$rc" -eq 1 ] || fail "expected refusal (1), got $rc"
    32	grep -q "roadmap-dashboard.sh" <<<"$out" || fail "refusal missing the regeneration instruction"
    33	
    34	# 2. Same range with the dashboard regenerated in it -> pass.
    35	cd "$R"
    36	echo "dash v2" > ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md
    37	git add ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md
    38	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q -m "regen dashboard"
    39	FIXED="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
    40	cd "$root"
    41	bash "$GUARD" "$R" "$FIXED" "$BASE" || fail "regenerated range should pass, got $?"
    42	
    43	# 3. Legacy-mode repo (no releases marker) -> guard is inert even on a ledger-only range.
    44	cd "$R"; : > .pdda-mode; cd "$root"
    45	bash "$GUARD" "$R" "$LEDGER_ONLY" "$BASE" || fail "legacy-mode repo must not be guarded, got $?"
    46	cd "$R"; echo "ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases" > .pdda-mode; cd "$root"
    47	
    48	# 4. New branch (all-zero remote sha) -> no range, no refusal.
    49	bash "$GUARD" "$R" "$LEDGER_ONLY" "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" \
    50	  || fail "new-branch push must fall through, got $?"
    51	
    52	# 5. Non-ledger range (docs commit only) -> pass.
    53	cd "$R"
    54	echo "readme" > README.md
    55	git add README.md
    56	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q -m docs
    57	DOCS="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
    58	cd "$root"
    59	bash "$GUARD" "$R" "$DOCS" "$FIXED" || fail "non-ledger range must pass, got $?"
    60	
    61	# 6. Usage error -> exit 2.
    62	rc=0; bash "$GUARD" >/dev/null 2>&1 || rc=$?
    63	[ "$rc" -eq 2 ] || fail "expected usage exit 2, got $rc"
    64	
    65	
    66	# 7. GH-315: jog-queue-only ledger write, renderer in-sync, no dashboard change -> ALLOW
    67	# (jog rows have no dashboard projection; a no-diff regen is the expected outcome, not a
    68	# dropped-row symptom). The fixture needs a renderer whose --check passes to reach the
    69	# guard's no-drift branch at all.
    70	mkdir -p "$R/utils"
    71	printf '#!/usr/bin/env bash\nexit 0\n' > "$R/utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh"
    72	chmod +x "$R/utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh"
    73	cd "$R"
    74	git add utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh
    75	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q -m "add passing renderer"
    76	RENDERED="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
    77	{ echo "-- dump v3"
    78	  echo "INSERT INTO settings(key, value) VALUES('generation', '9');"
    79	  echo "INSERT INTO jog_queue VALUES('seed');"
    80	  echo "INSERT INTO op_receipts(op, target_gid) VALUES('jog-add', 'seed');"
    81	} > releases.sql
    82	git add releases.sql
    83	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q -m "jog-only ledger write"
    84	JOG_ONLY="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
    85	cd "$root"
    86	bash "$GUARD" "$R" "$JOG_ONLY" "$RENDERED" \
    87	  || fail "jog-queue-only ledger write must pass the no-drift branch (GH-315), got $?"
    88	
    89	# 7b. Same shape, marathon fan-out: `releases marathon add` writes marathons + issue_refs
    90	# alongside the op_receipts/generation rows test 7 already covers, and neither new table is
    91	# projected by the renderer -> ALLOW. issue_refs is included because `marathon add` creates
    92	# that row itself whenever the tracking URL is new, so a marathons-only allowance would still
    93	# refuse the common case.
    94	#
    95	# WHAT THIS PROVES, EXACTLY: that the shell classifier accepts the canonical marathon dump
    96	# lines. It does NOT prove the two tables lack a dashboard projection — the fixture renderer
    97	# above is a stub that always exits 0, so no real rendering happens here. That claim rests on
    98	# `roadmap list` being a flat SELECT * FROM roadmap_items (utils/py/releases_app.py:3554) with
    99	# issue_url a denormalized TEXT column, not a foreign key (:605). See #474.
   100	cd "$R"
   101	{ echo "-- dump v3b"
   102	  echo "INSERT INTO settings(key, value) VALUES('generation', '10');"
   103	  echo "INSERT INTO issue_refs(global_id, url) VALUES('ref-1', 'https://example.invalid/1');"
   104	  echo "INSERT INTO marathons(global_id, tracking_ref_gid) VALUES('mar-1', 'ref-1');"
   105	  echo "INSERT INTO op_receipts(op, target_gid) VALUES('marathon-add', 'mar-1');"
   106	} > releases.sql
   107	git add releases.sql
   108	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q -m "marathon-only ledger write"
   109	MARATHON_ONLY="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
   110	cd "$root"
   111	bash "$GUARD" "$R" "$MARATHON_ONLY" "$RENDERED" \
   112	  || fail "marathon-only ledger write must pass the no-drift branch, got $?"
   113	
   114	# 8. Control: a roadmap_items data change in the same no-drift shape still refuses.
   115	# This is the red control for 7 AND 7b — it is what proves the allowlist opened the gate for
   116	# unprojected tables only, and not for everything.
   117	cd "$R"
   118	{ echo "-- dump v4"; echo "INSERT INTO roadmap_items VALUES('r1');"; } > releases.sql
   119	git add releases.sql
   120	git -c user.email=t@t -c user.name=t commit -q -m "roadmap-only ledger write"
   121	ROADMAP_ONLY="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
   122	cd "$root"
   123	rc=0; out="$(bash "$GUARD" "$R" "$ROADMAP_ONLY" "$RENDERED" 2>&1)" || rc=$?
   124	[ "$rc" -eq 1 ] || fail "expected no-drift refusal (1) for non-jog data, got $rc"
   125	grep -q "NO diff" <<<"$out" || fail "expected the no-diff refusal message"
   126	
   127	echo "== GH-243 ALL PASSED =="

    83	# GH-243: deterministic dashboard-staleness guard — a roadmap-ledger write (releases.sql/.db)
    84	# without a ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md regeneration is refused before any suite spends a second.
    85	# Releases-mode scoping, the range walk, and the refusal text live in the guard script (testable
    86	# on its own); it only ever ADDS a refusal and never substitutes for the gate below.
    87	if [ -x "$REPO/githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh" ] && [ "${#_real_ref_pairs[@]}" -gt 0 ]; then
    88	  _guard_args=()
    89	  for _pair in "${_real_ref_pairs[@]}"; do
    90	    IFS='|' read -r _gl _gr <<<"$_pair"
    91	    _guard_args+=("$_gl" "${_gr:-0000000000000000000000000000000000000000}")
    92	  done
    93	  bash "$REPO/githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh" "$REPO" "${_guard_args[@]}" || exit 1
    94	fi
    95	
    96	# A docs-only commit is still checked, but by the deterministic documentation gate instead of the
     8	every change leaves the harness **more durable, more reversible, and less duplicated** than it found
     9	it, and that the three stay in balance:
    10	
    11	- **Durable** — it removes the root cause and the next planned change builds on it, rather than being
    12	  torn out when the obvious next feature lands.
    13	- **Reversible** — the cost of being wrong is known and bounded before the change lands. A change
    14	  nobody can undo is a bet, not a fix, and gets treated as one.
    15	- **DRY** — nothing canonical lives in two places where it can drift. One source of truth, and every
    16	  other surface is a pointer or a projection of it.
    17	
    18	**Do not build a new layer, module, or sub-system when an existing piece of code can be extended
    19	easily, logically, and safely.** Extending an existing abstraction beats standing up a parallel,
    20	siloed system, even when the parallel path is faster to write today — the parallel path is what
    21	later has to be kept in sync, and what silently drifts when it isn't. If the existing abstraction
    22	genuinely cannot carry the new case, say so in one line, with the reason, before forking.
    23	
    24	These three pull against each other, and that tension is the decision, not a problem to average
    25	away: the most durable fix is often the least reversible, and collapsing two near-duplicates is a
    26	DRY win that can widen the blast radius. Name the trade and pick; do not split the difference by
    27	building both.
    28	
    29	This section is canonical. `AGENTS.md` owns how it is applied to a given change — the reversibility
    30	scale (§3) and blast-radius sizing (§4) — and principles 6, 7, and 10 below are the build-time and
    31	done-time expressions of it. Where another doc restates this, that doc is the copy and this is the
    70	
    71	6. **Build durable, not band-aid.** Durable means it removes the root cause and the next planned change builds on it — not a patch torn out when the obvious next feature lands. A band-aid is wasted work unless a demo strictly needs one, and a demo band-aid is tagged for removal so it isn't silently inherited.
    72	
    73	7. **Least code that clears the bar.** The `tick` coordination kernel uses Node's standard library. The repository also ships `package.json` and `package-lock.json` for Acorn-based source analysis. Prefer reusing or extending what exists; the smallest change that stays correct, contained, and durable wins. Net-new code is a cost to justify. Deleting code counts as progress.
    74	
    75	8. **Honest; the operator decides.** Surface what failed and why — never mask a stall as success or an escalation as a stall. A headless turn self-repairs within a bounded exit-code menu (`exit 3` stall, `exit 4` escalated-by-design, `exit 6` containment revert), then stops; it never loops forever or silently swallows an error. Destructive actions require explicit authorization.
    76	
    77	9. **Docs support resumable work (PDDA).** ROUTER points to the governing contracts; the RELEASES DB owns this repository's roadmap ledger, and ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md is its generated view. Linked PROJECT documents hold plans, decisions and handoff detail; CHANGELOG records dated outcomes. Resume execution using those documents together with the relevant runtime state and evidence. If current documentation contradicts the implementation, correct it or explicitly record the unresolved discrepancy.
    78	
    79	10. **Done means verified.** "Done" is `validate.sh` green, the relevant PDDA checks passing, and any relay review returning `Approved` — not work that looks finished. An unverified success claim is itself a low-quality signal.
    80	
    81	11. **Issue-first; every non-trivial change has a signal stream.** Any change beyond a 2–3 line fix opens a GitHub issue first, then gets a `GH-<number>` in-repo pointer doc, then lands. The issue is the machine-queryable signal stream; the `PROJECT/**` doc is the execution surface of record. Genuinely trivial edits (≤2–3 line fixes, typos, path repoints, doc-only one-liners) are exempt.

   135	  failure-prone enough to tax the whole system: worktree isolation, path claims, the driver lock,
   136	  multi-round handoff, escalation, and resume. Short, single-shot tasks land fine but prove nothing.
   137	  Four rules follow, and they are load-bearing:
   138	
   139	  1. **Exactly one long-horizon marathon is in flight at a time.** When one lands, choosing the next
   140	     is a real decision, not a default. It is named in the roadmap ledger's **Immediate next-up**
   141	     (read `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`; the RELEASES DB is the source of truth since the
   142	     `ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases` flip) as the marathon, so an agent arriving cold can tell which item
   143	     is the load and which items are riding alongside it.
   144	  2. **Prefer the marathon-shaped candidate.** *Marathon-shaped* means: decomposable into many items
   145	     with an identical transform, a per-item pass condition a machine can check, and a plausible way
   146	     to break the harness. GH-10 (73 unaudited suites, one mechanical adoption each) is the
   147	     archetype. Picking a non-marathon-shaped item over a marathon-shaped one of comparable value
   148	     needs a stated reason — write it in the ledger entry, not in a commit message.
   149	  3. **Only real work.** Never manufacture a marathon to keep the system busy, and never build a
   150	     synthetic workload that cannot damage anything — a run with no blast radius does not surface
   151	     the failures that matter. If nothing genuinely needed is marathon-shaped right now, **the
   152	     correct state is idle**. Say so plainly and do the smaller work; an idle gap is honest signal,
   153	     a fabricated marathon is noise that costs real tokens.
   154	  4. **The point is the failures.** A marathon that completes cleanly and teaches nothing is a
   155	     weaker result than one that escalates and names a defect. Report what broke; do not smooth it.
   156	
   157	- **The RELEASES DB is two subsystems behind one CLI** (`utils/py/releases_app.py`): the GH-32
   158	  release ledger and the roadmap ledger (`roadmap_items`). Since the `ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases`
   159	  flip (GH-169/GH-238/GH-243) and ROADMAP.md retirement (GH-269), the DB is the roadmap's source of truth in THIS repo: park intake
   160	  with `releases roadmap add` (or `hq park`), and read with `roadmap list` / `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md`.
   161	  `roadmap sync` is for legacy-mode repos only and no-ops here — it mirrors markdown and would delete
   162	  `add`-parked rows. Never hand-edit `releases.sql` or `releases.db`. Merge conflicts on the dump have
   163	  a one-command resolver (`utils/releases-merge-resolve.sh`). The whole contract, including what a real
   164	  merge conflict looks like: [RELEASES-DB-FAQS.md](RELEASES-DB-FAQS.md).

     1	#!/bin/bash
     2	# test/gh239-hq-status-releases-mode.sh
     3	# Tests GH-239: hq status and rollup read from releases DB in releases-mode repos.
     4	
     5	set -eu
     6	source test/_setup.sh "GH-239" || { echo "setup failed"; exit 1; }
     7	
     8	root="$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)"
     9	
    10	export XYZ_PATH="$root"
    11	export HQ_SEARCH_ROOTS="$WORK"
    12	
    13	# 1. Legacy repo
    14	mkdir -p "$WORK/repo-legacy"
    15	cd "$WORK/repo-legacy"
    16	git init >/dev/null 2>&1
    17	touch .pdda-mode ROADMAP.md
    18	cd "$root"
    19	
    20	out="$(bash utils/hq/hq.sh status "repo-legacy")"
    21	grep -q "ROADMAP ✓" <<<"$out" || fail "legacy repo missing ROADMAP ✓"
    22	
    23	# 2. Releases-mode repo (healthy)
    24	mkdir -p "$WORK/repo-healthy/utils/py"
    25	cd "$WORK/repo-healthy"
    26	git init >/dev/null 2>&1
    27	echo "ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases" > .pdda-mode
    28	cp "$root/utils/py/releases_app.py" "utils/py/"
    29	python3 utils/py/releases_app.py init >/dev/null
    30	# Wait 1s to ensure ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md is newer than releases.db
    31	sleep 1
    32	touch ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md
    33	cd "$root"
    34	
    35	out="$(bash utils/hq/hq.sh status "repo-healthy")"
    36	grep -q "RELEASES-DB ✓ (dashboard ✓" <<<"$out" || fail "healthy repo missing RELEASES-DB ✓ or dashboard ✓"
    37	
    38	# 3. Releases-mode repo (broken DB)
    39	mkdir -p "$WORK/repo-broken"
    40	cd "$WORK/repo-broken"
    41	git init >/dev/null 2>&1
    42	echo "ROADMAP_SOURCE=releases" > .pdda-mode
    43	# No releases.db or CLI here
    44	cd "$root"
    45	
    46	out="$(bash utils/hq/hq.sh status "repo-broken")"
    47	grep -q "RELEASES-DB ✗ (releases-mode declared, but releases.db or CLI missing)" <<<"$out" || fail "broken repo missing degrade message"
    48	
    49	echo "  PASS: status tests"
    50	
    51	# 4. Rollup aggregation — assert on the CONTENT the releases branch emits and on the exit
    52	#    status, not just the pre-python echoes (those print even when the branch is broken).
    53	echo "  Testing rollup.sh..."
    54	export HQ_PDDA_REGISTRY_DIR="$WORK/test-rollup/registry"
    55	mkdir -p "$HQ_PDDA_REGISTRY_DIR"
    56	echo -e "repo-healthy\t\t\t\t" > "$HQ_PDDA_REGISTRY_DIR/registry-test.tsv"
    57	export PDDA_REGISTRY_JSON="[{\"repo\": \"repo-healthy\", \"path\": \"$WORK/repo-healthy\"}]"
    58	export AGY_BIN="echo"
    59	export MARATHON_SCAN_BIN="echo"
    60	export MARATHON_LIVE_BIN="echo"
    61	export RELEASES_CYCLE_BIN="echo"
    62	export HQ_OBSIDIAN_VAULT="$WORK/vault"
    63	
    64	# seed one open roadmap item so the releases branch has something real to aggregate
    65	cd "$WORK/repo-healthy"
    66	python3 utils/py/releases_app.py roadmap add --issue-num 7 --issue-url "https://gh/7" \
    67	  --title "Rollup item seven" --created 2026-08-25 --doc-path "PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-7-x.md" >/dev/null \
    68	  || fail "could not seed roadmap item"

    70	
    71	rollup_rc=0

ANSWER: Choose C. Keep renderers as on-demand views, but stop tracking their Markdown output and remove the dashboard staleness gate.

FINDINGS:

- [Blocker] The claimed “decisive fact” is not literally true: `AGENTS.md` still directs cold agents to read only `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` for the marathon’s Immediate next-up, with no CLI alternative (`AGENTS.md:139-143`). `GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md` also names the dashboard as the generated resumability view (`GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md:77`). C requires fixing those routes, not merely ignoring them.

- [Should] The CLI pairing is strong evidence that committed Markdown is unnecessary as state, but not that the rendering is useless. The renderer adds summaries, status tallies, and extracted links that the human CLI does not provide (`utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh:123-246`); it is useful for a cold agent and GitHub reader. C preserves that utility without making every ledger write carry a second mergeable projection.

- [Blocker] Under C, HQ must stop treating an absent file as stale. It currently emits `LOCAL_DASHBOARD_STALE=yes` for both a genuinely stale artifact and a deliberately absent one (`utils/hq/hq-lib.sh:357-362`), then displays “dashboard ✗ stale” (`utils/hq/hq.sh:153-157`). This is a trivial, bounded fix—report “on-demand/untracked” or omit freshness—not evidence that cross-repo status is wrong. But it is mandatory in the same change.

- [Should] The churn is real engineering cost, not aesthetics. It creates an additional concurrently written path beyond the ledger, and the merge-resolve test explicitly proves both sides conflict on `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` before regenerating it (`test/gh57-live-merge-resolve.sh:260-285`). Ledger conflicts can still occur, but C removes a separate, mechanically derived conflict and the required synchronization work.

- [Should] B correctly repairs #474’s immediate classifier defect, but preserves the underlying design: a push blocker must execute a renderer and distinguish a harmless non-projection write from a silently dropped row. The present guard admits its table allowlist is incomplete and structurally wrong (`githooks/dashboard-staleness-guard.sh:154-161`). A machine-readable dropped-row exit would improve it, but only strengthens a costly coupling C eliminates.

- [Pass] C is compatible with existing test architecture: the dashboard suite already skips artifact-synchronization assertions when the artifact is absent (`test/roadmap-dashboard.sh:34-38`). Retain renderer tests using explicit temporary output, and delete only committed-artifact/staleness assertions.

- [Pass] Reversibility favors C over A. B is cheapest to undo but leaves the ongoing cost. C can be reversed by removing ignore rules, regenerating, recommitting, and restoring the guard from history. A deletes the useful local/GitHub-readable projection surface and costs more to restore. This is a Costly but reversible change; it is the DRY trade the principles require you to name and choose (`GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md:8-27`).

RECOMMENDATION: Choose option C.
